# Evaluation protocol (fixed before any training)

Written on 2026-10-04, before step 0. Changes to this file after results are seen must be listed in the "Changes" section at the bottom with the reason, and the original numbers must still be reported.

## Task

Zero-shot intent classification with many labels. At test time the model receives only the label names (and, from step 0 on, label descriptions produced by a fixed procedure). It never receives labeled examples of the test task.

## Test sets (held out)

| Set | Language | Labels | Size | Role |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Banking77, test split (`mteb/banking77`) | English | 77 | 3,076 | primary test |
| HWU64, full test | English | 64 | ~1,076 | second English test |
| xSID `lt` test, nat:lt (if the license allows), otherwise SIB-200 `lit_Latn` | Lithuanian | varies | small | Lithuanian test |
| MASSIVE test, selected locales | multilingual | 60 | per locale | multilingual check |

No example, label set or derived data from these families (Banking77 and its translations, HWU64, MASSIVE, SLURP, SNIPS, xSID, MTOP) is used for training, prompt design or model selection.

## Development sets

All design decisions (description prompt, k, hard-negative mining, learning rate, checkpoint choice) are made on development data only: CLINC150 without the `banking` and `credit_cards` domains, and held-out intents from the training families (SGD, TOP, ATIS). Test sets are run once per finished configuration, never inside a tuning loop. From step 1 on, the development sets are the ones listed in "Changes" (entry "before step-1 training").

## Metrics

- Accuracy and macro-F1 on the full test set, 95% bootstrap confidence intervals over examples.
- recall@k of the retrieval stage (k = 1, 5, 10, 20).
- Latency in ms per example on a Kaggle T4, batch size stated.
- For training runs: 3 seeds, mean and range.

## Label descriptions

Generated once per label set by an open model with a permissive license, using one fixed prompt designed on development data. Descriptions for test label sets are not edited after looking at test errors. All prompts and generated descriptions are published.

## Leakage checks (results go into the README)

1. Overlap of intent names between training data and each test label set.
2. Near-duplicate search between training texts and test texts (embedding similarity, top matches reviewed by hand).
3. Synthetic data: prompts never contain test label names; generated banking intents are removed or reported separately.
4. Base models: training-data statements of every base model (e.g. BAAI/bge-m3, rerankers) are checked for the test datasets and documented.

## Baselines reported next to every result

BAAI/bge-m3 cosine to the label name, BAAI/bge-m3 with descriptions, Laya (default and `head_max_len=512`), and published numbers of other systems with their protocol stated (sample size, number of classes).

## Success criteria

- Main goal: accuracy >= 0.75 on the full Banking77 test, zero-shot, with no Banking77 data in training.
- Strong goal: >= 0.78.
- The result must not get worse on the second English test or on the Lithuanian test compared to bge-m3 with descriptions.
- If a goal is not reached, the real numbers are published anyway.

## Changes

- 2026-10-04, after step 0 (run 20261004T155947Z). No change to test sets, metrics or success criteria.
  - Step 0 result: prompt v2 chosen on CLINC dev (0.733 vs 0.724 for v1, 0.676 names only). Test, evaluated once: Banking77 0.660 (names only 0.647; delta +0.013, CI includes 0), HWU64 0.597 (names only 0.459). Banking77 recall@10 rises from 0.932 to 0.959 with descriptions.
  - The plan's step-0 threshold (Banking77 >= 0.69) is not met. Decision: no further description-prompt iterations; continue to step 1 (trained reranker over the top-k), because the remaining Banking77 errors are confusions between close intents and recall@10 = 0.959 leaves room for a reranker. The step-0 numbers above stay in all reports.
  - Leakage check: 8 of 242 generated example phrasings for Banking77 (3 of 223 for HWU64) exactly match a test message; they are short generic questions (for example "Why did my transfer fail?"). Added before step 1: every final result is also reported with descriptions stripped of the quoted example phrasings, to show the result does not depend on this overlap.
- 2026-10-04, before step-1 training (no step-1 result seen). No change to test sets, metrics or success criteria. Reason: the step-1 data audit (licenses, leakage, dev design) changed which data can be used. Implemented in `notebooks/step1_build_training_data.ipynb`.
  - Development sets for step 1 (replace "held-out intents from the training families (SGD, TOP, ATIS)"). Each pool is scored separately with its own label set: CLINC150 dev = the 60 intents of the travel, work, home and kitchen_and_dining domains, test split only (1,800 rows); DSTC11 Track 2 insurance development set (22 intents); SGD intents that are absent from the SGD train schema (7 intents: AddAlarm, GetAlarms, FindHomeByArea, RentMovie, ShareLocation, FindTrains, GetTrainTickets; CC BY-SA, evaluation only); synthetic held-out domains (10%, drawn from the synthetic domains whose name has bge-m3 cosine < 0.80 to every other synthetic or real training domain) and held-out intents inside training domains (8%); MultiWOZ 2.2 held-out intent names (15%; small pools are merged into `mixed_dev`). Dev labels with a training label name at bge-m3 cosine >= 0.80, or with a training label text (name and description) at cosine >= 0.85, are flagged, and dev is also reported on the strictly unseen labels (neither flag).
  - CLINC auto_and_commute, utility, small_talk and meta (60 intents, all splits) are now training data. The step-0 dev numbers on 120 intents are therefore not directly comparable with step 1; the step-0 baseline is re-scored on the 60 dev intents.
  - ATIS is dropped from the development families (LDC license). TOP is not used in mix A; TOP v1 only in mix B and only after its license is read; TOPv2 is not used (no-derivatives agreement).
  - Training mixes: A (default: CLINC training domains, MultiWOZ 2.2, synthetic data from Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct), A+ (adds Bitext and HINT3), B (adds SGD train and/or TOP v1). Every reported result names its mix.
  - Leakage rules for training data. A training row is removed if it matches a test message exactly after normalisation or at bge-m3 cosine >= 0.95; rows between 0.90 and 0.95 are reviewed by hand and removed when judged a duplicate. A training row that repeats a dev message (exact, or cosine >= 0.95) is removed as well. A training label whose quoted example phrasing matches a test message (exact, or cosine >= 0.95) is trained on its description without the examples; the frozen description itself is not edited.
  - Test sets audited for names and texts (checks 1 and 2): Banking77 test, HWU64 test, MASSIVE en-US test, xSID v0.7 `en` and `lt` test, SIB-200 `lit_Latn` test; with TOP v1 (mix B) also the MTOP English test. nat:lt is audited only after its license is confirmed (its label set is the xSID set). Every set that could not be audited is listed in the step-1 manifest.
  - HWU64 and MASSIVE are no longer fully independent of training: the CLINC training domains utility, meta and small_talk cover several of their intents (for example alarm, weather, time and date, definition, calculator, traffic, taxi, volume, repeat, yes/no, jokes, greetings). HWU64 and MASSIVE are therefore also reported on the subset of test labels that have no training label at bge-m3 name cosine >= 0.80 and no word-set relation to a training label name (list in `step1_audit_test_labels_seen.csv`).
  - Descriptions for all step-1 labels use the frozen step-0 procedure (prompt v2, sha `c82a0e75bac6`, the same generator commit, fp16, greedy, batch 16), and the 120 CLINC descriptions are the step-0 ones. The run stops instead of falling back to another model revision or to 4-bit.

The ONE/TWO decision was made on DEV only and frozen before any test file was loaded (step0b_decision_20261004T175329Z.txt, sha256 cc3ad541a2fe161ff45535949dc83227b226d77c8e3b71946bf467b0a0a13010); test numbers are in step0b_test_info_20261004T175329Z.txt.
  - Primary decision (protocol rule, DEV accuracy): ONE (FINAL); best ONE reranker_all_labels 0.706, best TWO m3_dense_top10_reranker 0.710. Secondary information only: latency-gated decision TWO, PRODUCT v1 TWO.

  - Leakage check 4 done (base models): the training-data statements of BAAI/bge-m3 and BAAI/bge-reranker-v2-m3 do not mention Banking77, HWU64, MASSIVE, SLURP, SNIPS, xSID or MTOP; exposure through web-crawl pre-training text cannot be excluded (sources in step0b_results.md).
- 2026-10-04, architecture for step 1, fixed before any step-1 training (based only on the step-0b DEV results above).
  - Inference is two-stage: fast retrieval of the top-k labels with an embedding mode, then precise rescoring of those k labels with a cross-encoder. Reason: the only single-stage config that matched the two-stage accuracy on DEV (cross-encoder over all labels, 0.706 vs 0.710) needs 112 ms per message at 150 labels on a T4 and grows linearly with the number of labels, above the 40 ms budget; the bge-m3 built-in ColBERT and sparse modes gave no gain on DEV (combined 0.679 vs dense 0.676), so they are not used.
  - Open question, decided after step 1 on DEV only with the same rule (ONE if ONE >= TWO - 0.01): whether both stages share one set of weights. Step 1 trains (a) a separate cross-encoder initialised from BAAI/bge-reranker-v2-m3, used after bge-m3 retrieval (two weight files), and (b) one shared-backbone model with an embedding mode and a cross-encoder mode (one weight file). If (b) passes the rule, the product is one model; otherwise v1 ships (a) and (b) is revisited by distillation in v1.1.
- 2026-10-04, training mix for step 1, chosen before any step-1 data or training result. Step 1 is built and trained on mix A+ (mix A plus Bitext, filtered, and HINT3, filtered), to give the model more distinct real intents. The licenses of both additions allow commercial use of a trained model (CDLA-Sharing-1.0, ODbL produced work); only a redistributed copy of their data stays share-alike, so the step-1 data files are not published as a whole. All step-1 results name mix A+.
- 2026-10-04, step-1 selection rows, fixed before any step-1 training result (only the step-1 data run 20261004T184143Z was seen). No change to test sets, metrics or success criteria. The checkpoint of each variant, k and the ONE/TWO decision are computed on the CLINC dev rows that are not flagged `near_test` (near-duplicates of a message of an audited test set; 20 of the 1,800 rows in that data run: 17 MASSIVE en, 2 xSID en, 1 HWU64), so that no test-like row is used for model selection. The numbers on all 1,800 rows are reported next to them.
- 2026-10-05 02:50, data v2, logged before the data v2 and v1fix runs (no step-1 training result on data v2 or v1fix seen; seen so far: the data run 20261004T184143Z and a DEV-only GPU pilot on that data, see the next paragraph). No change to test sets, metrics or success criteria.
  - Seen before this entry, DEV only: a GPU pilot (600 steps per variant on the v1 data, no test cell in the notebook) gave clinc_dev accuracy at k = 10 of 0.718 for the untrained reranker, 0.791 for (a) and 0.780 for (b), and eager single-message latencies of 59 to 113 ms (k = 3 to 20). These numbers are used only for throughput, memory and the k rule below; they are not a step-1 result and are not reported as one.
  - Parser bug in the v1 data run: in 52 of the 100 synthetic domains Qwen wrote lines as "intent_name: name: gloss"; the parser named all of them "intent name" and the duplicate-name filter removed them (1,591 of the 1,657 duplicate-name drops). The v1 data (mix A+, 1,307 training labels) is not used for training; its numbers stay in the record. The fixed parser has a regression test on the cached v1 outputs and relative stop gates (no label key above 3% of parsed intents, at least 90% of domains with 20 or more parsed intents, no artifact names, duplicate-name drops at most 25% of parsed intents).
  - Recipe v2 (mix A+v2), as specified in docs/work/data_v2_DESIGN.md (section "Revised changes (FINAL)") and implemented in notebooks/step1_build_training_data.ipynb: a frozen reviewed list of customer-service domains (list 2, 112 entries, sha256 f6ca6e11b487edf87569b3c33213730fd86952bfa9fa7a18b185b6df109c4db5; 110 used), objects per domain, intents per object in the form "name | meaning", seeded sampled messages with attribute bundles, sibling lists and style references from real training rows of other domains, a Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct judge that keeps only messages assigned to their gold intent (intents with acceptance below 0.35 or fewer than 6 kept messages are dropped), stricter dedup, and a synthetic blocklist extended with billing, bill(s), billed, invoice(s), invoiced, tax(es), overcharg*, undercharg*, surcharg*. Synthetic intents with name cosine >= 0.85 to any audited test label name are dropped (both recipes). Descriptions are unchanged (frozen prompt v2, greedy).
  - Disclosures: the task format of the recipe (single-domain, fine-grained customer-support intents, at least 2 problem intents out of 6 per object, a 25% problem-share warning gate) comes from the project brief; it is a design choice, not derived from data. For reference, the problem-type label share in the real sources is 0.05 (DSTC11 insurance), 0.07 to 0.19 (Bitext, name only to name plus description), 0.06 to 0.09 (HINT3) and 0.02 to 0.05 (CLINC dev); every source's share is written to the data manifest. The exclusion of personal-assistant scenarios keeps the HWU64 and MASSIVE scenarios unseen and was derived from their public label sets. The service-domain list (a prompt input) was written for the project along with the code; domain names appear as metadata (domain column, dev pool names, description context); every training and dev text is either from the listed datasets or generated by Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct. Banking77 is additionally reported on its seen and unseen labels, as HWU64 and MASSIVE already are.
  - Leakage: every training row at bge-m3 cosine >= 0.90 (or an exact match) to an audited test message is removed automatically (stricter than the earlier manual review of 0.90 to 0.95); dev rows at >= 0.90 are flagged near_test and excluded from selection. 'unreviewed' counts training rows only and must be 0.
  - Hard negatives and false negatives (data notebook, trainer EX mask and (b) in-batch mask use the same rule): same domain: same name or label-text cosine >= 0.92; different domain or source: label-text cosine >= 0.88 or name cosine >= 0.85; for synthetic gold rows the cross-domain negative slots take only labels with name cosine < 0.80 to the gold. At least 10 same-domain candidates (K_MIN_IN_DOMAIN 10, both recipes).
  - Generation budget: GEN_TIME_BUDGET_MIN = 220 minutes (the largest budget that keeps the whole run inside 4 hours, measured in the smoke estimate). The separate estimate pass is skipped: the smoke-based projection (about 1,300 synthetic training labels at 175 minutes) is below 2,000, so the pre-registered rule would raise the budget to this limit in any case. The full run records its own projection and the realised label count. Filters are never loosened.
  - Recipe v1fix (mix A+v1fix): the v1 recipe with only the parser fix, the leakage auto-removal, the false-negative rule, K_MIN_IN_DOMAIN 10 and the 0.85 test-name filter. It exists for one pre-registered comparison: a DEV-only pilot trains variant (a) on v2 and on v1fix for the same number of steps; v2 is used for the main run unless v1fix is at least 0.010 better on clinc_dev rows not flagged near_test (paired bootstrap reported). Smoke runs of the data notebook used the GPU (about 1.3 GPU hours in total).
- 2026-10-05 03:10, training v2, logged before any training run on data v2 or v1fix (no such result seen). No change to test sets, metrics or success criteria. Implemented in notebooks/step1_train.ipynb (sources in tools/step1_train_build/src, specification docs/work/step1_train_SPEC.md and docs/work/v2_PLAN.md sections 2 to 5).
  - Sampler: per-source caps of 48 rows per real label and 10 rows per synthetic label per epoch; per group 4 of the 7 negatives from the first 10 same-domain entries of the stored hard negatives when available, the rest from the remaining top 20, then the documented top-up. EPOCHS 2, TRAIN_DEADLINE 7.5 h. Equal steps: after calibration both workers exchange their measured seconds per step and set the same total number of steps (the smaller of 2 epochs and what each fits before the deadline); both calibrations go into the decision file.
  - False-negative rule for the trainer's mask and the (b) in-batch InfoNCE mask: identical to the data notebook (entry 02:50), checked by a self-test in every run.
  - k rule (replaces the earlier rules): per variant, k is the smallest k in (3, 5, 8) whose accuracy on the clinc_dev selection rows (not near_test) is at least the best accuracy over these k minus 0.002; (3, 5, 8) are the k that meet the 40 ms single-message target on a T4 with the optimised runtime measured in the latency bench (docs/work/latency_bench_REPORT.md; p50 19, 25 and 37 ms at 150 labels; k = 10 needs 42 ms). The same k set is the checkpoint key (best accuracy over these k at each periodic DEV evaluation). The unconstrained best k over (3, 5, 8, 10, 20) is reported next to the chosen one. An eager latency table is measured before the freeze and written into the decision file as information. The ONE/TWO rule is unchanged (ONE if acc_b >= acc_a - 0.01 at the chosen k values).
  - Selection rows: clinc_dev rows not flagged near_test (cosine >= 0.90 to an audited test message in data v2); a run with more than 10% flagged rows stops before training.
  - Modes: PILOT (training and DEV only, the test cells refuse to run) and PILOT_COMPARE_DATA (variant (a) on data v2 and on data v1fix with a common MAX_STEPS; rule in the 02:50 entry). The main training session (variants (a) and (b), one seed) is the only run that opens the test sets, once.

Implemented in notebooks/step1_build_multilingual.ipynb (design docs/work/ml_DESIGN.md; review, changelog and final check docs/work/ml_*.md).
  - Goal extension: the model is also evaluated in Lithuanian, Polish and other European languages. English guard: on Banking77 en and HWU64 en the multilingual model is compared with bge-m3 with descriptions as before; an English-only training on the same data is a step-2 ablation if the GPU quota allows, reported when run.
  - Provenance: translations and native generations only from EuroLLM-9B-Instruct-2512 (Apache-2.0, sha def82454026b0353f12e48a0de081a9ed380d91e) and Helsinki-NLP OPUS-MT models with Apache-2.0 or CC-BY-4.0 licenses (pinned commits, attribution in the ML manifest). No text written in the working chats is in the training or dev data. Not used: NLLB, SeamlessM4T, Aya, Tower, Gemma, Llama-family, Qwen2.5-3B, Qwen2.5-72B, fastText language ID. vLLM was measured and is not faster on a T4; HF transformers generate is used.
  - Training languages: lt and pl (messages and label names by EuroLLM; full label texts as EuroLLM name plus OPUS-MT description), lv, de, fr, es, it, ru (OPUS-MT, greedy). Up to 6 source rows per training intent for lt and pl and up to 3 for the other languages, stratified over sources; native lt and pl generations for at most 600 intents per language (temperature 0.8, top_p 0.95, 3 messages per intent), at most 25% of each language's kept rows. A 4.0 h deadline caps the volume by measured speed. Quality gates stop the run: EuroLLM fp16 corpus chrF on 300 pinned Tatoeba Challenge pairs per language must be at least the OPUS-MT chrF minus 5 for lt and pl, plus limits on empty, copied, wrong-script and wrong-language outputs. Per-row filters as listed in the ML manifest.
  - Dev (design and selection only): CLINC150 dev-60 (15 rows per intent) and the synthetic held-out-domain pools (8 rows per intent), translated by a different system than the training rows of that language: lt and pl by OPUS-MT, de by EuroLLM, and sk (held-out dev language, never trained, no registered test) by OPUS-MT. Selection stays on the English clinc_dev rows; per-language dev is reported.
  - Leakage: every candidate translation, native and dev row is audited against all splits of every test family in its language (MASSIVE 1.1, xSID v0.7 including the German dialect files, MTOP, SIB-200, MInDS-14) by exact match and bge-m3 cosine >= 0.90, and cross-lingually against all splits of the English test families (Banking77, HWU64, MASSIVE en-US, xSID en, MTOP en, MInDS-14 en, SIB-200 eng) with a threshold of clip(median cosine of our own translation pairs in that language, 0.80, 0.90). A hit drops the whole translation group in every language (dev groups too). The English source rows of groups with an exact or same-language hit are also left out of English training in every run that uses the multilingual data (manifest key leakage.english_rows_to_exclude, applied by the trainer and counted in the decision file); the DEV-only data-comparison pilot runs before the multilingual data exists and uses the English rules only, equally for both arms. Quoted example phrasings in translated label texts are audited the same way.
  - Registered multilingual tests (test only; never used for training, dev or design; English label names and the frozen English description procedure for every label set; no per-language tuning): MASSIVE 1.1 test (60 intents, 2,974 rows per locale) for en-US (anchor), the translated-train locales pl-PL, lv-LV, de-DE, fr-FR, es-ES, it-IT, ru-RU and the held-out locales nl-NL, pt-PT, ro-RO, sv-SE, da-DK, nb-NO, fi-FI, hu-HU, sl-SL, el-GR (reported per locale and as means of the two groups); xSID v0.7 test (16 intents, 500 rows per language) for lt (the headline Lithuanian test), en, de, it, nl, da; MTOP test for en, de, fr, es; MInDS-14 (14 intents, natural transcribed banking queries) for pl-PL, de-DE, fr-FR, es-ES, it-IT, ru-RU, cs-CZ, nl-NL, pt-PT, en-US; SIB-200 (7 topics, all 1,004 rows per language) for lit_Latn, pol_Latn, lvs_Latn, est_Latn, deu_Latn, fra_Latn, spa_Latn, fin_Latn, eng_Latn. NaLiBaSID nat:lt only with a written data license obtained before any multilingual result, otherwise listed as not run. A Banking77 translation into Lithuanian is run only if it is machine-translated by a third system, post-edited by a native speaker and frozen with its hash before any multilingual result; otherwise it is listed as not run. HWU64-family seen and unseen labels are reported for MASSIVE as for HWU64.

Readable names must be unique within a set.
  - Topic areas in the step-0 context "a virtual assistant (topic area: X)": MASSIVE the scenario (equal to the step-0 HWU64 prefix rule; every MASSIVE label that is an HWU64 label must give the identical step-0 HWU64 prompt); xSID the family before '/'; the seven SNIPS names without a family get no topic area, so their context is exactly "a virtual assistant", the step-0 rule for a label without a prefix (an earlier draft that used the label's own name as topic area was dropped after review, before any GPU run); MTOP the domain of the intent (most frequent domain over its English test rows; IN:GET_LOCATION: people); MInDS-14 "mobile banking" (equal to the step-0 Banking77 context, checked); SIB-200 "news topics". Disclosure: the hints "news topics" and "mobile banking" for MInDS-14 and the inventory rules above were chosen when the code was written; they are prompt inputs and label-space definitions, not data text. Every description text is generated by Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct.
  - Stop rules (no fallback): before generation, the label sets fail closed (a pin mismatch, a loader failure, a count other than the registered one, a MASSIVE intent outside its scenario or a readable-name collision stops the run); the GPU setup must be 2x Tesla T4 with device map ['0', '1'] and the loaded generator commit must equal the pin; a reproduction check regenerates the first step-0 batch of 16 CLINC dev descriptions (dev labels) and stops the run before any test description is written unless all 16 raw outputs are identical to step 0 (ALLOW_REPRO_MISMATCH = False; changing it needs a new entry here before the run). A stopped or corrected run is logged here as a re-run.
  - Leakage report (report only, after the freeze, descriptions never edited because of it): the quoted spans of each set's descriptions (step-0 QUOTE_RE) against the test messages of the same set in every registered locale, by exact match (step-0 ASCII key on English rows, Unicode key on all rows) and bge-m3 nearest cosine; spans that are the label name or have fewer than 2 tokens are flagged and counted separately.

No change to the test sets, metrics or success criteria. Design documents: docs/work/v11_DESIGN.json (research, plan and critique; the critique's revisions are adopted below).
  - Disclosures: v1.1 was designed after the v1 Banking77 and HWU64 test aggregates were known (entry 14:10). During the v1.1 research pass the first columns of step1_results.csv were printed, which include the test rows' retrieval recall of bge-m3 and of the (b) embedding mode. No test text or prediction was opened. The justification of the embedding adapter below rests on DEV only: (b) embedding recall@10 on clinc_dev 0.989 vs 0.971 for frozen bge-m3, on clinc_dev@lt 0.972 vs 0.960.
  - Definition of the v1.1 product (chosen in the working chat, the project's working conversation where decisions were taken and results reported, 2026-10-05): ONE model means ONE file: the cross-encoder backbone plus a LoRA adapter (rank 32, alpha 64, attention and MLP projections of all 24 layers, about 14M parameters) that is active only in the embedding pass (normalised CLS) used for retrieval. Inference stays two passes; memory is one backbone instead of two.
  - Arm ONE-0: the v1 (a) cross-encoder (seed 0) is frozen unchanged; the adapter is trained by MSE to the frozen bge-m3 CLS vectors (English messages, training label texts, and every multilingual row mapped to the bge-m3 vector of its English source), with an InfoNCE term of weight 0.25 in the last half of training. Gate R5 (non-inferiority, DEV): the fused pipeline accuracy of ONE-0 on the row-pooled 13 English DEV pools other than clinc_dev (mixed_dev excluded) must be at least that of the v1 cross scores fused with bge-m3 cosines minus 0.005; clinc_dev recall@8 at least 0.962; clinc_dev@lt recall@8 at least bge-m3's minus 0.005. If R5 fails, one rerun at rank 64 is allowed (on the cluster); if that fails too, v1.1 ships v1 (two files) and the one-file goal moves to v1.2.
  - Arm A1: the cross-encoder is retrained with the v1 recipe plus three changes: a language-first sampler (the group language is drawn first with en 0.50, lt 0.125, pl 0.125 and the other languages sharing 0.25; the message is a translated or native row of the same gold label in that language, English only if the label has none; a CPU gate checks the realised shares en 0.50 +- 0.03, lt 0.125 +- 0.02, pl 0.125 +- 0.02 before training), one epoch with full learning-rate decay, and a uniform running average of the weights over the last 40% of steps, shipped without checkpoint selection. The same adapter recipe is then trained on it. Rule R4 (DEV): A1 replaces ONE-0 if (1) the row-pooled accuracy over the lt and pl DEV pools (clinc_dev@lt, clinc_dev@pl and the synthetic @lt and @pl pools) is at least ONE-0's plus 0.010, and (2) the row-pooled accuracy over the 13 English pools is at least ONE-0's minus 0.010. Paired bootstrap intervals are reported. sk is a control language: the gains of A1 over ONE-0 are also reported as (lt minus sk) and (pl minus sk); a language-specific effect is claimed only from those.
  - Score fusion for every config: final score = cross logit + w x cosine of the model's own embedding over the top-k candidates. w is chosen from {0, 5, 10, 15, 20, 30, 40, 60} by the row-pooled mean accuracy over the 13 English DEV pools other than clinc_dev (mixed_dev excluded); the w curve is reported. k by the existing rule within (3, 5, 8). The latency of the chosen config is measured on DEV inside the same session with the optimised runtime (single message, 150 DEV labels) and reported against 40 ms.
  - Not in v1.1 (deferred to v1.2, each would need its own entry): sibling-aware label descriptions, hard negatives re-mined with the trained model, the 37 missing synthetic domains, teacher-ensemble distillation, a smaller student model.
  - Seeds 1 and 2: retrain the shipped cross mode (A1, or (a) if ONE-0 ships) and its adapter per seed with the identical config (the cluster or next week's Kaggle quota); the shipped model stays seed 0; mean and range are reported.
  - Test: one look for v1.1 (Banking77, HWU64 and the registered multilingual sets) with the v1.1 primary config as the headline whatever the test shows, plus w = 0 and descriptions without examples as information; all earlier looks (step 0, step 0b, step 1 v1, the v1 multilingual test, and the recall columns disclosed above) are listed next to it; no best-of-v1-or-v1.1 claim. Before that look, the test code is ported to the adapter and fusion runtime, checked by a CPU smoke and a DEV reproduction gate, and its notebook sha256 is logged here.
- 2026-10-05 17:45, v1.1 implementation details, logged before the v1.1 GPU run (no v1.1 result seen: only CPU smoke runs of the v1.1 notebook with tiny random models and generated data, whose numbers mean nothing). No change to the test sets, metrics or success criteria. This entry completes the 16:05 entry with the choices of the implementation that the plan does not fix; where its wording differs, this entry says so. Notebook notebooks/step1_v11.ipynb sha256 ae6c63341d4c161d52dd66be5f1c6986ad6101a4a11c075fee1cdc04a16dbdcc (the GPU kernel copy kaggle/step1v11/step1_v11.ipynb is identical; sources tools/step1_v11_build/; review docs/work/v11_REVIEW.md, changes docs/work/v11_FIX_CHANGELOG.md).
  - (a) Adapter inputs: every English training message (teacher: its own bge-m3 vector), every training label text, every multilingual translation whose English source row is a training row of the same label (teacher: the vector of that source row), and every native lt and pl generation of a training label (about 3,319 rows; they have no English source row, so their teacher is the bge-m3 vector of their own text). Translations without a training source row are skipped and counted. Teacher vectors: frozen bge-m3 at the pinned revision, CLS of the last layer, L2-normalised, fp16, max length 512, except texts longer than the student's window (128 tokens for messages, 192 for label texts), which get their vector at that window.
  - (b) Adapter recipe, identical for both arms and not tuned: LoRA rank 32, alpha 64 on q, k, v, o, fc1 and fc2 of all 24 layers, as forward hooks that are active only in the embedding pass; AD_STEPS optimizer steps of 64 messages (a seed-0 permutation of the adapter messages); the label texts of a step are the unique gold labels of its messages; loss = MSE + 0.25 x InfoNCE from step floor(0.5 x AD_STEPS) on, where MSE is the squared L2 distance between the normalised student and teacher vectors summed over the 1,024 dimensions (= 2 - 2 cos) and averaged over the step's messages and label texts, and InfoNCE is message to in-batch label texts, temperature 0.02, with the v1 false-negative mask; AdamW lr 5e-4, betas (0.9, 0.999), no weight decay, linear warmup over 5% of the steps, then linear decay to 0, gradient clipping 1.0, fp16 autocast with a GradScaler, dropout set to 0 in the frozen backbone, non-reentrant gradient checkpointing; the end state is kept (no selection). AD_STEPS is the largest multiple of 100 in [2000, 3000] whose projected A1 worker time fits the 4.35 h worker deadline with a 0.25 h margin. The projection uses the speeds measured by a GPU preflight on GPU 0 before the workers start (adapter step and DEV cross pair, times 1.15) and v1's measured cross-encoder step (2.93 s x 1.07). The same value is used for both arms; if 2000 steps do not fit, the run stops before training. The value is written to the decision file.
  - (c) Decision rows: in every pool, the rows not flagged near_test (translated pools: the flag of the English source row), for w, k, R4 and R5. This extends the 2026-10-04 selection-row entry from clinc_dev to every pool; all-row numbers are reported next to them.
  - (d) w is chosen at k = 8 (the largest k of the k rule); then k follows the 03:10 rule with the fused scores at that w.
  - (e) R5 compares ONE-0 and V1M3 each at its own rule-chosen (w, k); R5 (ii) and (iii) use the decision rows. The three R5 quantities are also reported for A1, as information only (R5 gates ONE-0 only).
  - (f) If R5 fails, no one-file model ships from this session, also when R4 would choose A1 (A1 carries the same adapter recipe); the 16:05 path applies (one rank-64 rerun, else v1 ships).
  - (g) A1: warmup 85 steps (5% of 1,704, the v1 WARMUP_FRAC); the deadline logic may refit the total steps at steps 20 and 100 or stop the training early; the A1 cross-encoder and both adapters are kept in any case. Completeness: a run in which A1 did not train all 1,704 steps of its single epoch with the LR decayed to 0 and a uniform average over at least 682 steps (40%), or in which an adapter did not complete its AD_STEPS steps, is PROVISIONAL, not FINAL. So is a session with a single GPU, a failed GPU preflight, or a V1M3 that does not reproduce the frozen v1 pipeline (top-1 agreement at k = 8 with the stored v1 DEV scores on clinc_dev below 0.99, or clinc_dev selection-row accuracy at w = 0 and k = 8 more than 0.003 from 0.8211). If ONE-0 fails, A1 is stopped once its cross-encoder is saved.
  - (h) Earlier statements changed by the 16:05 plan, recorded here explicitly: R4 decides between the arms on the lt and pl DEV pools and the 13 English pools, which changes the 03:35 statement that selection stays on the English clinc_dev rows (for v1.1); the language-first sampler of arm A1 changes the 14:10 statement "reported, not changed" (v1 stays as it was trained).

Its zero-shot configuration (w 60, k 5, v2 label texts) is the reference row Z of every DEV task.
  - Methods (training-free; examples are embedded by the model's own embedding pass, adapter on, message length 128): P = retrieval by the cosine to p_l = normalise(t_l + sum of the label's example vectors); K = retrieval by r = (1 - a) cos(q, t_l) + a agg_i cos(q, e_li), agg in {max, mean, lse with tau 0.05}, a in {0.25, 0.5, 0.75, 1.0} (a label without examples uses the label-text cosine); KX = K's retrieval at K's chosen (agg, a), with the cross-encoder reading for each candidate label its text without the generated quoted examples (the data notebook's strip_examples) followed by the min(3, n) examples of that label nearest to the message, each in double quotes (inner double quotes made single, at most 25 words). Every variant: final = cross logit + w x r over the top k by r, w in {0, 10, 20, 30, 40, 60, 80, 120, 160, 240}, k in {3, 5, 8}; ties as in v1.1 (retrieval: the lower label index; fusion: the first maximum in retrieval order, float64).
  - DEV example pools (never a test family): the CLINC150 training split of the 60 clinc_dev intents (`clinc/clinc_oos` config plus, `plus/train-00000-of-00001.parquet` at commit 155b9c710419136e17307b80d0a13e68cd46b4ec, sha256 30188119cf9f86fc9db27e1c22442d091cb5cb0913c9496f945fe11e7a02a28f, CC BY 3.0; unused in training and DEV so far: data v2 manifest, dropped rows "train/validation rows of dev-domain intents (not used anywhere)", 7,200 rows). The notebook reads only the 60 dev intents (a pyarrow row filter on their intent ids; no other intent's text reaches Python) and asserts 100 rows per intent; a sanity gate stops the run if the model's zero-shot top-5 retrieval does not contain the mapped label of at least half of the 6,000 rows (a wrong id-to-name mapping). Other DEV pools: a seeded split of their own decision rows: per label, the rows sorted by row_id are permuted with numpy default_rng([seed, crc32(pool|lang|label_id)]); the pool's example size P is the largest N in (2, 5, 10) such that at least 80% of its labels have at least N + 3 decision rows; each label's examples are the first min(P, n - 3) rows (N uses the first N: nested pools), the evaluation rows are the next 30. CLINC examples: per intent the 100 training rows in file order permuted with default_rng([seed, crc32("clinc_train|" + label_id)]), the first 10 form the pool, N uses the first N. Seeds 0, 1, 2. The entry of 23:33 names "the training rows of the other DEV pools" as example pools; these pools are held-out labels without training rows, so their examples are a seeded split of their own decision rows (rule above); this replaces that wording. The CLINC training rows were not audited against the registered test sets (that needs test texts, which this DEV session never reads); they are crowd-sourced paraphrases of the 60 clinc_dev intents, which are not test intents, and the effect on the choice is expected to be negligible.
  - Near duplicates: per seed, an evaluation row is dropped when its English source text has a bge-m3 (BAAI/bge-m3 at 5617a9f61b028005a4858fdac845db406aefb181, CLS, normalised, max length 512) cosine >= 0.90, or an exact match after the data notebook's norm_text, to the English source of any drawn example of the same task (the 10-row pools, all labels); the same rows serve N = 2, 5 and 10. Counts are reported; all-row accuracies are information.
  - Tasks. Decision: EN = clinc_dev (CLINC train examples), bitext_dev, dstc11_insurance, sgd_unseen (own splits; each must have P = 10, else the run stops; with data v2 all four have P = 10); LTPL = clinc_dev@lt and clinc_dev@pl with the English CLINC train examples. Information: clinc_dev@de and @sk (English examples); the synth_dev pools (own splits, N up to P; hint3_dev has P = 0 and is not used); every translated pool with a seeded split of its translated rows, once with the translated examples and once with their English source rows (same rows; the difference is reported with a cluster bootstrap). mixed_dev and pools with fewer than 4 labels are not used. Label texts are the English texts for every language.
  - Decision quantity of a configuration: Q = mean over N in (2, 5, 10) and seeds (0, 1, 2) of (0.5 x EN + 0.5 x LTPL), with EN and LTPL the row-pooled accuracies of their tasks' evaluation rows.
  - Rule within a variant: the parameters (P: w; K: agg, a, w; KX: w at K's agg and a) maximise Q at k = 8 (ties: max, mean, lse; a ascending; w ascending), then k is the smallest k in (3, 5, 8) with Q at least the best over these k minus 0.002, at the chosen parameters.
  - Rule between variants (exactly three configurations; the zero-shot model is the reference row and cannot be selected, since the track reports a few-shot method by registration): V = P; K replaces V if Q(K) - Q(P) >= 0.003 and the lower end of the 95% bootstrap interval of the paired difference is above 0; then KX replaces V if Q(KX) - Q(V) >= 0.005 and the lower end of its interval is above 0. Bootstrap: 2,000 resamples, seed 0, of clusters = (pool, English source row), so a clinc_dev row, its lt and pl translations and its instances under every N and seed move together; Q is recomputed per resample with the cluster weights. The same intervals with clusters = (pool, gold label) are reported (information). The intervals are conditional on the chosen parameters: K's parameters are the maximum of Q over 120 settings and P's over 10 on the same rows, and the interval does not adjust for these grid sizes, so the rule is somewhat optimistic in favour of K (and KX); the variants differ only in inference cost and one configuration goes to the look.
  - Reading of the 3-configuration rule: the comparison has exactly three configurations, P, K and KX; P is the baseline (the default of the rule) and the zero-shot model is a reference row that cannot be selected. One configuration, chosen with N = 2, 5 and 10 pooled, serves every N, so the N = 2 rows run at a configuration chosen partly on N = 5 and 10 (as registered under P11 (a)).
  - One configuration for every N (audit item P11 (a)): the rule's choice (variant, aggregation, a, w, k) is the primary few-shot configuration at N = 2, 5 and 10 alike; only it runs in the look; the other variants are DEV information and are never called the few-shot result.
  - Reproduction gate (before any decision number): the zero-shot pipeline of the model recomputed on all 1,800 clinc_dev rows must agree with the stored v1.1 DEV scores (`v11_dev_scores_one0_adapter.parquet`, sha256 071ba413c8e1821dcf01878842f5e8bf5104022abeabbcb924c741b3b6dc4fc6; their decision-row accuracy at w 60, k 5 is 0.8176, recomputed from the file) in top-1 at (w 60, k 5) on at least 0.99 of the rows, and the decision-row accuracy must be within 0.003 of the stored one; otherwise the run stops and nothing is decided.

  - B-7 rule. Fixed under the general delegation, before any probe number (verbatim): "diacritic-stripping augmentation enters the public model only if the paired loss on the LTPL pools (clinc_dev@lt and @pl with diacritics stripped, same rows) is at least 0.03 with the cluster interval excluding 0; typo augmentation is not added to public model v1; ALL CAPS is solved by the library." Provenance: the answer recorded in `docs/work/QUESTIONS_ru.md` (question 13) fixes the loss of at least 0.03 for LTPL without diacritics; the interval clause and the pool list (clinc_dev@lt and @pl) were added under the delegation in the build task of 2026-10-06 afternoon, after that answer and before any probe number. Mechanics (trigger T-DIA): it fires when, on the 1,466 LTPL decision rows, accuracy with diacritics removed minus clean accuracy is at most -0.03 and the upper end of its 95% cluster interval (2,000 resamples, seed 0, clusters = (base pool, English source row)) is below 0; a missing, failed or NaN quantity, a PROVISIONAL run or a failed gate never fires. Reading of the rule, made consistent with question 11 of the same answer (typos and diacritics are checked as public-model variants after the test, chosen on DEV only by a rule logged before the test, entries E1 to E4): this probe adds no typo or ALL CAPS augmentation; its typo and ALL CAPS rows are information. Whether typo or diacritic variants of the public model are trained and chosen is decided only by the public-model entries E1 to E4 (logged before the look) and the not-worse rule NW-P. T-DIA sets the default diacritic-stripping share of public model v1 (`AUG_STRIP_P_LTPL`, the share of Lithuanian and Polish cross-encoder training groups whose message is stripped; in v1 the 0.15 share was about 1.2% of all cross-encoder groups; the retrieval adapter gets no stripped messages, SUMMARY_ru 2.1 B-7 (b)): 0.30 if it fires, else 0.15 (the recipe since v1); a variant with another share can still be tested under E1 to E4. ALL CAPS gets no augmentation; the library offers an opt-in `normalize_caps` (B-11 item 6: lowercase a message when at least 80% of its cased letters are upper case), off by default, and the probe's `caps_norm` row measures what that option recovers. If a noise setting differs from the recipe since v1 and the public model fails NW-P, the first rerun is the public model with every noise setting at its v1 value (its own entry; both records reported), as in AMENDMENTS_12b B-7. This rule replaces the B-7 triggers of AMENDMENTS_12b (stripping -0.05, typos -0.02, ALL CAPS -0.02) and the LTPL definition of SUMMARY_ru 2.1 B-7 (v); none of them was logged, and none is to be copied into the stage-1.2 S1 entry. LTPL here is clinc_dev@lt and clinc_dev@pl, for this run and for the stage-1.2 rerun alike. The PROTOCOL text governs the effect; the record's `effect_if_fired` and `b7_fixed` texts are labels. Nothing else in the record changes anything.
  - Which measurement decides T-DIA: the one on the honest model whose recipe the public model follows. If stage 1.2 keeps the v1.1 model, this run decides; a later probe of v1.1 on data 1.2 (B-5) is information and does not re-decide T-DIA. If stage 1.2 adopts a new model, the same module and rule are rerun on it (mode `noise_probe_s12`, at the w and k of its decision record, with the extension on; its own short entry) before the public-model entry, and that rerun decides; if that rerun is not run, not FINAL or INVALID before the public-model entry, T-DIA does not fire for public model v1 (share 0.15), and the v1.1 result is never used as a substitute.

Extension pools cut by the deadline or failed are listed and never change the status. The core record is written before the extension; a failure after the core record leaves the core record as the record of the run.   - The library is an implementation of the registered runtime, not a new configuration: it loads the one-file model (v1.1 layout and the stage-1.2 FINAL layout: backbone and head in fp16, the embedding adapter `r2intent_embed_lora.*` in fp32, active only in the embedding pass) and the two-file V1M3 fallback (bge-m3 at 5617a9f6... plus a cross-encoder; accepted only when an `anyintent.json`, an `embedder=` argument or a v1 checkpoint meta says so), and predicts with the frozen fusion: cross logit + w x cosine over the top-k labels by cosine, with w, k, the cascade threshold tau and the label text mode read from the model's files (`r2intent_meta.json`, an optional `anyintent.json` that may pin the PROTOCOL values). A one-file model without a fusion weight w is refused (w = 0 is a different configuration). Ties follow the DEV scorer (`r2intent_v11_lib.retrieve` stable order, `fused_pred` first maximum in float64). Label texts are "name: description" (name alone without a description), as for every registered number. `Prediction.margin` (best minus second-best fused score among the scored candidates) is a product field and is not used by any rule. It contains no training, data-building, mining or evaluation code.
  - Per-mode settings (interface addition): `anyintent.json` may carry `fusion_by_mode` = {mode: {w, k, tau}} for a label text mode other than the frozen one (for a release whose R8 outcome is v3s: the registered v2 settings of the same config, `configs.<adopted>.v2` of the stage-1.2 decision). `set_labels(..., label_text_mode=M)` then uses that block; without a block the frozen values apply as before.
  - Few-shot mode (PROTOCOL 2026-10-05 23:33 and 23:37): only for a model whose `anyintent.json` (or `r2intent_meta.json`) carries a `fewshot` block (the `chosen` keys of the few-shot DEV decision: variant P, K or KX, agg, a, w, k, lse_tau, m_text, ex_word_cap). `set_labels(labels, examples=...)` takes one list of example messages per label; without the block, examples are refused, and without examples the model is zero-shot exactly as before. The scoring functions (`anyintent/fewshot.py`) are a byte copy of the numpy part of `tools/fewshot_build/src/r2intent_fewshot_runtime.py` (sha256 ea0e80dde542abeec544f0dd7e7a08dbe10cc7c34c7164443355fda3fd4f64ac, the code of the few-shot DEV run and of the look), checked block by block by the build; the library calls its `score_batch` with its own vectors and cross pass. No few-shot setting is chosen by the library.

near ties are kept and counted; the job stops if more than 35% of a language's native rows would be dropped; counts in `native_filter`, realised shares of rows and training groups per language, natives per label, new vs base domains and the filter drop shares in `native_share`). Intent: "{name}". Similar intents in the same classifier: "{s1}", "{s2}", "{s3}", "{s4}", "{s5}". Write one plain declarative sentence of at most 20 words that says what a user with the intent "{name}" wants, so that it clearly does not fit the similar intents. Do not quote example messages, do not name the other intents, do not start with the intent name. Answer with the sentence only." It does not see the label's own v2 description. Label text: "name: <sentence>".
  - Variant B ("contrast prefix", max_new_tokens 64; prompt sha256 c12d6adf95cb36c68f554e29cc2c139a2695df101ac996638f15d933a3de924c): "You write label definitions for an intent classifier used by {context}. Intent: "{name}". Its current definition: {v2 description without examples} Similar intents in the same classifier: "{s1}", ... Write one plain declarative sentence of at most 25 words that says how the intent "{name}" differs from these similar intents. Do not quote example messages, do not name the other intents, do not start with the intent name. Answer with the sentence only." Label text: "name: <contrast sentence> <v2 description>" (the training-safe description without examples for flagged training labels), so the new sentence comes right after the name and survives longest_first truncation at 160 tokens.

  - **Shared sampler of C12 and HN12:** per label at most 48 rows (real) or 10 (synthetic) per epoch; for a synthetic label at most round(0.34 x drawn rows) rows with `has_name_word` (data 1.2's column: a content word of the label name that is not a word of its object; a small within-label swap, no accuracy claim), while other rows remain; realised shares before and after the cap and the relaxed-label count are recorded. Languages and messages by the branch sampler (v1 sampler for ONE0) with the v1.1 functions; for the language-first sampler the CPU share gate en 0.50 +- 0.03, lt 0.125 +- 0.02, pl 0.125 +- 0.02 stops ARM_DEV and SEEDS before any GPU work. Label-text mode per group: v2 for every English group (LABEL_MODE_MIX False; with True: v2 0.40, v3s-A 0.125, v3s-B 0.125, noex 0.20, name + first sentence 0.05, name 0.10); translated label modes (name_l, full_l) keep their v1 meaning. C12 and HN12 share rows, languages, messages and modes (fingerprints checked); only the negatives differ.
  - **Negatives (D14 as a mix, DECISIONS 2.6 item 7).** The pure in-label-set form is not run. Both lists are loaded: data 1.2's in-label-set column (`inset_neg_label_ids`: the bge-m3 top 15 of the gold's own label set after the false-negative mask; sets below 8 labels topped up from the same source) and data v2's list (`neg_label_ids`, B's own list). C12, 7 static slots: 5 drawn uniformly from the in-set ranks 1 to 7 after the mask and 2 drawn uniformly from the cross-set entries (label set differs from the gold's) of the data v2 list ranks 1 to 20 after the mask; a cross-set shortfall is filled from the in-set ranks 8 to 15; any further shortfall from the other stored in-set entries, then random labels of the gold's set, then any label outside the mask (counted). HN12: up to 4 mined (at most 3 uses of one negative per gold label per epoch over en, lt and pl) + 1 in-set (ranks 1 to 7) + 2 cross-set as above; mined shortfalls come from the in-set ranks 1 to 7. So both arms keep B's cross-set contrast (B: 0.736 of its negatives in the gold's set, about 5.15 in-set and 1.85 cross-set per group under its own sampler). Gates: the run stops before any GPU work if fewer than 95% of the stored non-top-up in-set entries are in the gold's set; no negative of a plan is ever inside the mask (asserted); the realised shares (C12: in-set share and static cross-set share; HN12: static cross-set share) must lie within 0.05 of the design values computed from the stored lists alone, else PROVISIONAL (checked on the C12 plan before training and on both trained plans after training); the share of data v2 list entries inside the new mask is information (the sampler drops them). Wording: the in-label-set part matches inference only for single-set label lists; 89% of reranker errors inside the gold domain is a clinc_dev figure (77% pooled); no size prior and no "+0.5 to +1.5" prior is claimed (expected 0 to +0.3 on single-set lists, multi-set behaviour at B's level); mining tops sets up to 9 labels, data 1.2 below 8. Mining (seed-0 plan of HN12; miner = B, its retriever's cosine at B's w; cosine-dominated): keys = every plan row (English) and every lt / pl group; candidates = every label of the gold's set (topped up to 9 from the same source); filters in this order, counted per filter, source, language and dense vs other: (1) the v1 false-negative rule, (2) the pair-file pairs that rule does not have (judge synonyms, and data-only rule pairs), (3) sigmoid(negative) >= 0.95 x sigmoid(gold) and label-text cosine >= 0.80 (a sigmoid ratio adapted by analogy from NV-Retriever; conservative, it may remove true hard negatives and reduce the HN12 gain; no accuracy claim); the filtered fused ranks 1 to 8 are stored and the sampler draws uniformly over them. The Jaccard of mined vs static in-set top 8 per source and language, the realised mined share, mined_short and mined_capped are written before R12. Caveat: self-mined negatives can hurt out of domain (NAIL; Pradeep et al.).
  - **False-negative mask (D01).** The trainer mask is the recomputed v1 rule OR the judge-synonym pairs of `step1_false_negative_pairs.parquet` OR the directed data EX of that file (no mirroring); a self-test compares the rule plus the synonyms with the file's directed EX (exact in the smoke, at most 0.5% of the data pairs per direction in the run, else PROVISIONAL); the stored in-set negatives inside the mask must be <= 0.5% of the rows.
  - **Translate-Distill (S04):** translated lt and pl training groups only (not other languages, not native generations, not English): loss = CE + 1.0 x KL(p_teacher || p_student), T = 1, p_teacher = B's cross softmax over the same candidates on the English source message (the sampler's own row, or the source of a same-label substitute) with the English v2 texts. Computed online in each training step by B's frozen fp16 cross (a deviation from the planned offline pass: same distribution, disclosed). Groups of unknown origin above 1% of the non-English groups stop the run before training; non-finite teacher logits above 0.1% stop the arm. Expected effect: LTPL 0 to +1 point, English 0; the L2 effect is not attributed separately. The MASSIVE motivation (0.568 vs 0.567) is a test aggregate of the multilingual entry (PROTOCOL 17:45, not 14:10) and is no longer used. A GPU preflight checks that the KD term changes the gradient. B-3: a CPU smoke forces diacritic stripping on every lt and pl message (ML_STRIP_P 1.0) for both samplers and asserts that every translated group keeps the teacher row of the unstripped plan, native rows stay native, KD stays on lt and pl only and no group has an unknown origin.
  - **Adapter (S05), ADAPTER_L1 False:** the v1.1 MSE recipe of A1 / ONE-0 (ADAPTER_V11: MSE to frozen bge-m3 vectors with the late in-batch InfoNCE of v1.1, 64 messages per step), rank 32, alpha 64, embedding pass only, AD_STEPS = 3,000 or the largest multiple of 100 in [2,000, 3,000] that fits at the measured speed, the same for both arms; the label side is v2 only, so R8 is biased toward v2.
  - **DEV scoring:** every pool of data 1.2 with the v1 caps; decision rows = not near_test (translations: the English source row's flag); per config retrieval by its own retriever on each mode's label texts, cross scores of the top 8; modes v2, v3s-A, v3s-B on every pool for B, C12 and HN12; noex and name capped at 10 rows per label for C12 and HN12 (diagnostics).
  - **Pool groups (D08, D09, D11, D21)** from the manifest as written by data 1.2: REAL (dstc11_insurance, hint3_dev, sgd_unseen, and nlupp_hotels_dev unless data 1.2 marks it information only as DECISIONS 2.6 data12 item 1 decides: the notebook follows the manifest's group and records a plan REAL pool made INFO without a PROVISIONAL reason), PIPE (bitext_dev and the synthetic pools), GUARD (DSTC11 banking and finance; veto only), CLINC (clinc_dev), INFO (abcd_dev after its label fix; out of every rule) and EXCLUDED (mixed_dev; it still counts in MULTI_EN, which only makes replacing B harder). en13_v11 = the decision rows of the 13 English pools of data v2 that v1.1 reported (every data v2 English pool except clinc_dev and mixed_dev). Rule quantities use PIPE without the fresh pools; LTPL = row-pooled lt and pl copies of the REAL, PIPE (no fresh) and CLINC pools (never GUARD, INFO or EXCLUDED copies); LTPL_v2 = those whose base pool exists in data v2; the values with the fresh pools are information. The stage-1.2 LTPL is not comparable with the v1.1 LTPL 0.7505. The run is PROVISIONAL, and stops before training, if a plan REAL or GUARD pool is missing or in another group (INFO for abcd_dev is recorded), or if REAL, PIPE, GUARD, CLINC, lt, pl, clinc_dev@lt or sk + de has no decision row or fewer than 100.
  - **w, k, tau (S13, S14, S15).** w from {0, 5, 10, 15, 20, 30, 40, 60} at k = 8 by EN_DEC (rule groups), ties to the smaller w; per-pool w curves, the cost of the chosen w for clinc_dev, LTPL, guard, lt, pl, de, sk (any below -0.005 named) and an information re-run of R12 at the smallest w within 0.002 of the best EN_DEC (`w_sensitive`) are recorded; the curve is flat, so w is not a claim. **k (DECISIONS 2.6 item 6 and the decision of 2026-10-06 in the working chat):** k is chosen from (3, 5) to keep the shipped speed (T4 p50 about 25 ms at k 5, 37 ms at k 8): the smallest k of (3, 5) whose pooled accuracy (EN_DEC rule groups + clinc_dev) is within 0.002 of the best of (3, 5), LTPL within 0.002, row-pooled sk + de within 0.002 and clinc_dev within 0.005 of their best over (3, 5); if none, 5. k = 8 is allowed only if its pooled accuracy is at least the best of (3, 5) + 0.005 and k = 8 meets the same clauses for LTPL, sk + de and clinc_dev (within their tolerances of the best over 3, 5 and 8); then K1 applies the registered 40 ms T4 rule to it (k_final). per_k stores every k's pooled, LTPL, sk + de and clinc_dev accuracy (k = 3, 5, 8; information) and the paired bootstrap of (k_chosen - k8); the K1 k-feasibility rule (21:37) uses the same clauses among the feasible k. Cascade tau (product option): the smallest tau in {1, 2, 3, 4} whose cascade accuracy on the pooled k-rule rows, on clinc_dev and on LTPL is each within 0.002 of the k-rule pipeline; none if k = 3 or none qualifies; `tau_by_k` for k = 3, 5, 8; K1, LOOK and packaging use tau_by_k[k_final]; reported numbers are without the cascade; B has no cascade (H1).
  - **Rule R12** (v2 texts; X at its own rule-chosen w and k, B at its shipped (60, 5) (H1b); every NaN fails its clause). Step 1: HN12 if EN_DEC(HN12) - EN_DEC(C12) >= +0.005, LTPL(HN12) >= LTPL(C12) - 0.005 and REAL(HN12) >= REAL(C12) - 0.005, else C12 (single-seed selection: no separate HN12-vs-C12 claim unless seeds 1 and 2 of both arms give a cluster lower bound above 0; step 1 has no multi-set clause, step 2 holds the multi-set clauses against B). Step 2, the candidate X replaces B only if: EN_DEC(X) >= B - 0.005; LTPL, LTPL_v2, REAL, lt, pl, clinc_dev@lt, row-pooled sk + de, MULTI_EN (clinc_dev, bitext_dev, synth_dev_seen_domain, sgd_unseen, hint3_dev, mixed_dev when present), MULTI_LTPL (lt and pl copies of clinc_dev and synth_dev_seen_domain), clinc_dev (if the switch applies) and the DSTC11 guard each >= B - 0.010; **en13_v11 (the 13 English pools of v1.1), de and sk each >= B - 0.010 (DECISIONS 2.6 item 4; the pooled de + sk clause stays)**; retriever recall@8 on clinc_dev and clinc_dev@lt >= B - 0.005; no single REAL, GUARD or CLINC pool below B - 0.020; and EN_DEC or LTPL gains >= +0.010 with the label-cluster 95% lower bound of that gain above 0. Option b adds: the one-sided 95% label-cluster lower bound of X - B above -0.010 on REAL, lt, pl, sk + de, clinc_dev@lt, clinc_dev, the guard, **MULTI_EN and MULTI_LTPL (H6)**; the label-cluster intervals of the en13_v11, de and sk clauses are recorded (information). Option c: B is kept. The decision records the outcome of all three options. **Rule (b) is what this entry fixes, and B most likely stays (v1.1 at w 60, k 5):** on the v1.1 DEV frames (label-cluster bootstrap, decision rows, measured X minus B, B's clinc_dev about 1 point optimistic; indicative, seed noise not included) option (b) with H6 adopts an equal model 0.0% of the time, the central C12 forecast (REAL +1.0, PIPE +0.5, lt 0, pl +0.2) 0.3%, a model truly +1 point everywhere 5%, +1.5 points 17%, +2 points 30%; it ships a real loss below 1 point in one guarded group at most 1.4% of the time (lt -0.8: 0.4%; sk + de -0.8: 1.4%; a multi-set loss: 0.2%; REAL -1.0: 0.2%), against 11 to 37% for option (a) and 2 to 21% for a+ (`docs/work/decisions/train_judge.md` 3.2). Disclosures: lv, fr, es, it and ru have no DEV guard; SIB-200 and the non-clinc assistant sets are protected only by the head_v2 row of the look; the margins (about 1 to 2 SE) tolerate sub-margin losses by design; the label-cluster MDE (about 0.016 on EN_DEC) is written next to every clause; the hint3_dev pool floor (-0.020 on about 121 rows) is noisy and is a large source of false vetoes; the "3x" error reduction cited for in-set negatives is Banking77-specific. If R12 keeps B, the headline is configs.B, the v1.1 shipped configuration at (60, 5) exactly (H1). The V100 p50 at X's k and at B's k is written to `stage12_latency_v100.json` (information); if X's k is larger, the summary names the latency cost.

Q = 0.5 x REAL' + 0.5 x PIPE (row-pooled decision rows; REAL' = REAL pools with at least 10 labels; PIPE without the fresh pools, including the 8 synth_dev_unseen pools). A variant passes if Q >= Q(v2) + 0.010 with the label-cluster 95% lower bound of Q(V) - Q(v2) above 0, REAL'(V) >= REAL'(v2) - 0.010, clinc_dev >= v2 - 0.005, LTPL >= v2 - 0.010, DSTC11 guard >= v2 - 0.010 and its 90th-percentile pair length at k = 8 is at most one 16-token bucket above v2's (a missing length fails), **and, as strictly as R12 (b) (H2): LTPL_v2, lt, pl, de, sk, row-pooled sk + de, clinc_dev@lt, en13_v11, MULTI_EN and MULTI_LTPL each >= v2 - 0.010, recall@8 on clinc_dev and clinc_dev@lt >= v2 - 0.005, no REAL, GUARD or CLINC pool below v2 - 0.020, and the one-sided 95% label-cluster lower bound of (variant - v2) above -0.010 on REAL', lt, pl, sk + de, clinc_dev@lt, clinc_dev, the guard, MULTI_EN and MULTI_LTPL**; every NaN fails. D18 (with integration fix I1): variant A is ineligible, decided from the data manifest's per-set counts before any DEV score, if its name-only fallback exceeds 2% in any DEV label set or external set of at least 50 labels, or 1 label in a smaller set; every registered external set (Banking77, HWU64, MASSIVE, xSID, MTOP, MInDS-14, SIB-200, CLINC banking + credit_cards) needs counts; if data 1.2 was built before K0 (part 2 of the external file pending), the part-2 counts of the `data_v3s_ext2` manifest (its data-1.2 manifest sha256 checked) are merged into the per-set counts; missing counts make A ineligible. If both pass: the higher Q; within 0.005, the shorter p90. If none passes: v2. D19: the data 1.2 truncation report (D20: DEV rows with their bge-m3 top 8 plus the gold) gives p90 pair lengths of 106 tokens for v2, 42 for v3s-A and 134 for v3s-B, that is 16-token buckets 7, 3 and 9, so v3s-B is predicted to fail the length clause (2 buckets above v2); the clause itself is measured at run time on each configuration's top-8 pairs. Reasons found before training stop the run before any training. A PROVISIONAL run is rerun at most once, unchanged except its logged fix (`--rerun-entry`, written into RERUN_ENTRY); both runs are listed; a second PROVISIONAL run adopts nothing. Deadlines: session 6.0 h, workers 5.3 h.

With fewer than 2 GPUs or under 60 GiB in total the 7B is used without a probe. Style references: real base training rows from source domains with bge-m3 name cosine below 0.80 to the seed, never from the D sources of the dom recipe (Bitext banking verticals, DSTC11 banking and finance, NLU++ banking, ABCD). Cleaning, the data 1.2 rules with the certainty-audit fixes D01 to D04: synonym mask from the judge confusions for mutual pairs only (each label loses at least 0.15 of its judged messages to the other); a pair at 0.30 both ways is merged only as a component of exactly two labels; the run stops if more than 15% of the pack labels are masked or more than 1% merged away; one yes/no description check per pack label (data 1.2 template) and a re-judge of every kept message against the label texts without examples are diagnostics only and remove nothing (answers saved, would-be removal rates reported). Every pack intent with bge-m3 name cosine >= 0.85 to any of these label names is dropped (PROTOCOL 23:33; the AMENDMENTS 5.5 idea of reporting instead of dropping is not used). Dev wins (same name, or name cosine >= 0.90, as a DEV label of a pool not trained in D); duplicates (exact, then cosine >= 0.92); a name in more than 3 synthetic domains (base domains count); a seed domain with fewer than 20 intents left is dropped.

If no k meets 40 ms, the rule-chosen k stays and the configuration does not claim the 40 ms target. Model D uses the headline's final k (frozen from the honest model). v1 (a) keeps its frozen k = 8 and claims nothing. p90 is reported, not gated.
  - Repeats near the budget (AUDIT P04): if a first p50 is within 2.0 ms of 40 ms, the 200-message single-message measurement is run twice more and the median of the three p50 values is the one used by the rule (all three, the SM clocks and the active throttle reasons before and after each run are recorded).

If the few-shot child fails or times out, K1 is run once more in full with a logged reason (its new zero-shot file replaces the first); if it fails again, the look runs with FEWSHOT_RUN False and a FEWSHOT_OFF_REASON naming both failures.

Each variant changes only what its own line lists against M0, the model that passed NW-P (P0, its WiSE mix, or a fallback run; a trained variant uses M0's recipe, and if M0 is a WiSE mix the variant is mixed with Z at the same a before it is compared). Every variant is scored at Z's frozen w 60, k 5 and tau null. Variants are information until chosen: their DEV numbers change no rule, no margin and no honest model; no variant is ever evaluated on a registered test set; only the one P chosen here is evaluated, once (E3). A variant whose settings are placeholders below is run only if they are fixed in a note under this entry before J7; otherwise it is recorded as not run for P v1. A variant that fails the rule is not kept and is not rerun.
    V-V3S (no training): M0's weights with label-text mode v3s-A at inference for every set (external texts `step1_v3s_external.json` ca415620... and `step1_v3s_external_clincbank.json` 1ebf21db..., frozen in J3a; DEV texts from data 1.2); eligible only by the D18 rule of the entry of 2026-10-06 23:44 (name-only fallback counts), decided before any P DEV score; target EN_DEC.
    V-E (e-mail wrappers): a share of the en, lt, pl and de cross-encoder groups (fixed in a note under this entry before J7) gets its message wrapped in a greeting and a signature in the message's language, drawn from a number of wrappers per language (fixed in the same note) generated by Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct (a09a35458c702b33eeacc393d103063234e8bc28, Apache-2.0) from a prompt written for this project (its sha256 logged before generation); no wrapper equals or contains a template of the noise probe (`docs/work/noise/noise_DESIGN.md` section 4); no text written for this project enters training; target: the EN_DEC and LTPL rows with the probe's e-mail change applied (module `r2intent_noise.py` ae087879...), pooled 0.5 / 0.5.
    V-HNLT: the HN12 recipe of the entry of 2026-10-06 23:44 (one pass, warmup 5%, linear decay to 0, uniform average of the last 40% of steps; up to 4 negatives mined by B with that entry's filters, 1 in-set, 2 cross-set; Translate-Distill with teacher B on translated lt and pl groups of honest sources) on data pub with P0's caps, noise, adapter rule and closed-inventory rule (inside an inventory the miner's filters are replaced by (2), and no Translate-Distill on closed-inventory rows), plus stronger lt and pl by a change fixed in a note under this entry before J7 (without that note V-HNLT, V-SOUP and V-ROUTE are not run); scored at w 60 like every variant (HN12's own DEV-chosen w was 30, entry 2026-10-07 01:55); target EN_DEC.
    V-SOUP (no training; only if V-HNLT was trained): the uniform average (0.5 / 0.5) of the cross-encoder weights of M0 and V-HNLT (both from bge-reranker-v2-m3 953dc6f6), the adapter by concatenation (rank 64); target EN_DEC.
    V-ROUTE (fallback form; only if V-HNLT was trained): messages detected as English go to V-HNLT, all others to M0; language detection by a detector named in a note under this entry before J7; two model files and a detector, so it is not the one-file product of the entry of 2026-10-05 16:05, and its T4 latency is measured in K2 before any latency claim; target EN_DEC.
    V-37: data pub plus the 37 domains of the frozen list that data v2 did not generate (E1), generated after the look by the GEN_37 = 1 path of the entry of 2026-10-06 21:47: 5 of them are drawn as fresh held-out DEV pools by the seeded rule before generation, the other 32 are training domains at the synthetic cap R_SYN 10; those 32 join domain_seen_by_P for V-37's own NW-P only; target: the 5 fresh pools (English, pooled), M0 scored on the same rows.
    V-TYPO: AUG_TYPO_P 0.10, one rule-based typo (the typing-error rule of the entry of 2026-10-06 17:18) in 10% of the en, lt and pl cross-encoder draws (AMENDMENTS_12b B-7), none in the adapter; target: the EN_DEC and LTPL rows with the probe's one-typo change (seeds 0, 1, 2), pooled 0.5 / 0.5. Reading of B-7: the 17:18 entry reads its verbatim sentence 'typo augmentation is not added to public model v1' as 'the probe adds none' and leaves the training and choice of a typo variant to this entry and NW-P; testing typos as a public-model variant was asked for in the working chat (QUESTIONS_ru lines 150 and 153).
    V-MIX: the training label-text mix: honest English groups v2 0.40, v3s-A 0.125, v3s-B 0.125, noex 0.20, name + first sentence 0.05, name 0.10; closed-inventory groups 60% their frozen inference text and 40% that mix; patch P allows LABEL_MODE_MIX True for this variant in FINAL pub only (FINAL asserts the decision's False today, and B's epoch-plan path sets every group to v2); target: the EN_DEC and LTPL rows scored with label names only (the probe's names-only change), pooled 0.5 / 0.5.
    V-TEACH: P0 plus listwise distillation from a teacher model (model, revision, rows and loss weight named in a note under this entry before J7, otherwise V-TEACH is not run; license read at the primary source before use; used only in training), loss CE + weight x KL(p_teacher || p_student) at T = 1; disclosure: the teacher's training data is not disclosed and may include registered test sets, which the model card states if V-TEACH ships; target: EN_DEC and LTPL pooled 0.5 / 0.5.
  - Variant rule VR ('not worse anywhere on P's DEV', QUESTIONS_ru line 177): quantities on P's DEV as for NW-P (same rows, same groups, the same domain_seen_by_P list for both models), each model at Z's frozen w, k and tau (V-V3S with its own label texts), plus VAL: the Banking77 carve-out, the HWU64 carve-out, the CLINC banking and credit_cards validation split and the MASSIVE dev split (mean of en-US and the 17 registered locales), each row-pooled, and VAL_mean their unweighted mean. Intervals: paired bootstrap, 2,000 resamples, seed 0, clusters (base pool, gold label) as the stage-1.2 rules (VAL: gold label). V is kept only if all hold: (a) V passes NW-P against Z; (b) EN_DEC, LTPL, EN_unseen, LTPL_unseen and VAL_mean of V are each >= M0 - 0.010 with the one-sided 95% cluster lower bound of V - M0 above -0.010 on each; lt_unseen, pl_unseen and de+sk_unseen each >= M0 - 0.015; GUARD and each VAL set >= M0 - 0.020; (c) on its target T, V - M0 >= +0.010 with the 95% cluster lower bound of V - M0 above 0; (d) every quantity exists. The label-cluster MDE on EN_DEC is about 0.019 (entry 2026-10-07 01:55), so VR cannot see smaller losses, and a variant without a measurable gain is not kept.
  - Order and choice: variants run after M0 exists, in this order, while the cluster hours allow: V-V3S, V-E, V-HNLT (then V-SOUP and V-ROUTE at no training cost), V-37, V-TYPO, V-MIX, V-TEACH; a variant not run is listed as not run. If no variant is kept, M0 ships; if one is kept, it ships; if several training-side variants are kept and the hours allow, one combined run of all kept training-side changes is trained and ships if it passes VR against M0 on every kept target, otherwise the first kept variant in the order above ships. V-V3S, if kept, is applied to the shipped model only after it passes VR against that model. The shipped P is frozen (package sha256) before its single test evaluation (E3).
  - Compute: FINAL pub runs on two V100s (P trains on GPU 0, Z is re-scored on GPU 1), so its V100-hours are twice its wall time. Planning figure for B's recipe: about 10 to 14 h of wall time and about 20 to 26 V100-hours per P training, --time 15:00:00 (DECISIONS_ru 2.7 item 9 and 4.4 step 13). The V100 speeds measured in J5 (0.867 s per cross-encoder step, 0.611 s per adapter step; `stage12_seeds_20261006T232026Z.json`, speeds.preflight) with PLAN_80's about 6,100 steps per epoch give about 2.9 h for the two epochs and about 1.0 h for 6,000 adapter steps (an estimate, not a measurement), so the planning figure is an upper bound. Patch P replaces the final_pub builder's deadlines (5.5 / 5.0 / 5.4 h, --time 06:00:00) by values from its own time plan, which S4 states before J8; each training variant or rerun costs about as much as P0, WiSE about 1.5 h (DECISIONS_ru 4.4 step 14). Model D's DEV report is information for P and changes no P setting (no early-warning switch is registered).

Checkpoint: the best of the periodic evaluations (step 0, every 400 steps, raised by v1's 12% rule, and the end), each the accuracy of the cross-encoder alone on the frozen bge-m3 top 8 (SELECT_K = 8, v1 (a)'s frozen k) of the clinc_dev rows of D's DEV that are not near_test, ties to the earlier step; a best checkpoint at step 0 is a warning in the record. Adapter: the v1.1 MSE recipe of ONE-0 (MSE to frozen bge-m3 vectors with in-batch InfoNCE at weight 0.25 from the second half of the steps, 64 messages per step), rank 32, alpha 64, AD_STEPS 3,000 pinned from the v1.1 record and never refitted, adapter seed 0.

  - Completeness: PROVISIONAL if the GPU preflight fails; the GPU is not a Tesla V100 or T4; the measured time plan does not fit the pinned AD_STEPS 3,000 or the 4.5 h session deadline; a data check fails (trainer-mask self-test; stored negatives inside the mask on more than 0.5% of the rows); the cross-encoder did not run every planned step or has no selected checkpoint; the adapter did not complete 3,000 steps; the cross pass with the adapter off differs from the cross-encoder; a DEV row has NaN scores or a pool is not scored. Reasons found before training stop the run before any training. A PROVISIONAL run is rerun at most once, unchanged except its logged fix (`--rerun-entry`). If D is not FINAL when the look is ready, the look runs with USE_MODEL_D False and a USE_MODEL_D_REASON (AUDIT P05).

    - intfloat/multilingual-e5-large-instruct at 274baa43b0e13e37fafa6428dbc7938e62e5c439 (MIT): mean pooling, "Instruct: {task}\nQuery: {message}".
    - Each embedder runs once with label names only and once with "name: description" texts. The task "Given a user message to a customer service assistant, retrieve the intent label that best describes what the user wants" is on the message side only. Inputs are truncated at 512 tokens; vectors are L2-normalised; prediction is the top-1 cosine.
    - Qwen/Qwen3-Reranker-0.6B at e61197ed45024b0ed8a2d74b80b4d909f1255473 (Apache-2.0) over the same bge-m3 top k (5), with "name: description" texts and the model card's chat template: its fixed system prompt, then "<Instruct>: {task}\n<Query>: {message}\n<Document>: {label text}". The score is logit("yes") minus logit("no") at the last position. The task is "Judge whether the Document is the intent label that best describes what the user wants in the Query".
  - Qwen3-Embedding-8B is a registered baseline of this cluster look (AMENDMENTS_12 T-2; RUN_OPTIONAL_Q3E8B True, set by the cluster builder).
  - The optional Qwen/Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct constrained classifier (llm_cls, on Banking77, HWU64 and xSID lt) is not run: RUN_LLM_CLASSIFIER False. Reasons:
    - It is not in the baseline list of line 44.
    - The look's time projection, which decides whether a registered (set, locale) may still start, does not include its cost, and its sets come first in the set order.
    - Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct also wrote every frozen label description.
  - Numerics rule (AMENDMENTS_12 T-2, RC-15), applied before the plan:
    - Every Qwen3 baseline scores the first 300 clinc_dev DEV rows in fp16 and in fp32. fp32 runs on one GPU where it fits, else split over both GPUs, else on the CPU.
    - A baseline with any non-finite fp16 output, or an fp16 vs fp32 top-1 agreement below 0.99, is reported "not run (fp16 numerics)" and is never rerun in another precision.
    - The check uses English rows only (disclosed).
  - Parity rule (AUDIT P12), before the plan:
    - Each embedder is compared with sentence-transformers on the same formatted query texts: mean cosine >= 0.999 on the first 32 clinc_dev rows.
    - Qwen3-Reranker-0.6B is compared with the model card's reference scoring: the same top-1 candidate for 4 messages x 8 candidates (32 pairs).
    - A failure is reported "not run (parity)".
  - Budget rule (AUDIT P13):
    - If the projected evaluation time (from the DEV preflight speeds) exceeds EVAL_DEADLINE_H (6.0 h), Qwen3-Embedding-8B is dropped before the plan ("not run (budget)").
    - No registered set is ever skipped for a baseline.
    - A resumed look reuses the first session's numerics, parity and not-run decisions.
  - Non-finite outputs inside the look (AUDIT P12):
    - They are counted per (set, locale, config); vectors are set to 0 and reranker scores to -1e9.
    - A cell with a count above 0 is flagged. It is never the best baseline of its (set, locale) or family, and no comparison against it is claimed.
    - Nothing is recomputed in another precision.
  - The BTZSC numbers quoted for Qwen3-Embedding-8B (AMENDMENTS_12 T-2) are English, bf16 and from another protocol. They are not comparable with these rows.
  - Information row, not a registered baseline (decided in the working chat on 2026-10-06, question 22; tails V39):
    - m3_desc_noex: bge-m3 cosine to "name: description" without the quoted examples. This is the label-text condition of the headline's noex row.
    - It is never the best baseline and selects nothing.
    - head_noex minus m3_desc_noex is reported as information.
  - Laya (line 44: default and head_max_len=512) is not in the look notebook. By the answer (a) to question 34, it runs once after the look in a separate script, registered in entry T3b of the same time.
  - Disclosure:
    - The instructions were written for this project along with the code. They are prompt inputs, not data.
    - Training-data statements (leakage check 4): the Qwen3 models' list of supervised data ends in "etc.", and the training data of multilingual-e5-large-instruct were not checked against the registered families; neither excludes them (Banking77, MASSIVE, and CLINC150, whose held-out banking and credit_cards domains are the confirmatory set), so these baselines' numbers may be optimistic.
