(ii) A setting PUB_BASE in the data notebook and in the ML notebook, 'v3' by default and 'v2' only in recipe pub. With 'v2': (a) the honest English training rows, labels and negative lists come from r2intent-data-v2-full and pass data pub's E4 removal and dev-wins rules like every row; the DEV freeze and the dev-wins rules read the data 1.2 DEV (dev e629fab4655a4986dc07b6d6754cc021d87fc254849d6d71c9a822d327e50aa6) and the ML 1.2 DEV copies (dev 486fa49f9e2ae4ade2d6755300d4be9367d0a6087110c1363a71ff648d9608de) from a second staged input; the data dom description cache is read with its own base check against data 1.2. (b) The ML notebook in recipe pub copies ML v2's honest training rows (r2intent-step1-build-multilingual, manifest 559980c8...) unchanged, with no ML 1.2 per-label rule (ROWS_PER_INTENT_REAL, `ml_cells/n1_settings.py` line 29) and no ML 1.2 native rule (lines 180 to 182); it maps their src_row_id to the new English row ids through base_row_id ((c)) and keeps the ML 1.2 DEV copies; the rows of the new public sources follow the ML pub rules as with 'v3'. (c) Ids: the data notebook renumbers every row (row_id = arange), so no file is joined across the two sources on row_id before renumbering (3,264 of the 16,460 data 1.2 DEV row ids equal data v2 training row ids). Every row carries base_space ('v2' or 'v3') and base_row_id, its row id in that source; honest training rows carry base_space 'v2' and their data v2 row id, DEV rows keep base_space 'v3' and their data 1.2 row id, and every join on base_row_id also matches base_space (asserted). A1's Z gate, NW-P and (10b) read DEV rows only, so their id chain is the data 1.2 chain used today. (d) Label columns: every column that `r2intent_s12_lib.py` requires (V3_LABEL_COLUMNS and label_columns_problems, lines 222 to 233: label_text, label_text_noex, label_text_v3s_A, label_text_v3s_B, name_first_sentence, name, label_set_id) is filled for the honest labels from the data 1.2 label file (labels 473ee43513645dc6ed87760ade114de94484c7b825811ed0e9cf4125b35ddf5a) by label id, with the v2 text as fallback; the 3 data v2 training labels that have no data 1.2 label row and every fallback are counted. (e) The smoke builds both PUB_BASE values on stand-ins; it checks that recipes v3, dom and pub with PUB_BASE 'v3' stay byte-identical, and with 'v2' that the honest ML rows equal ML v2 row for row apart from the remapped src_row_id and that the honest English rows equal data v2 apart from the E4 and dev-wins removals (counted).
  - (4) The levers of P_full. Every lever that adds training rows (the D pack, V-37, V-IND) builds on data pub and ML pub without changing any of their rows, labels or negative lists (asserted, as for the D pack), runs only after the look (each job refuses to run unless R2INTENT_LOOK_DONE equals the sha256 of the complete, non-smoke look manifest), passes E4's removal and the dev-wins rules of data pub, and is recorded with counts in its manifest. The other levers change groups at sampling time or the loss, store no text and need no further audit. Each added layer draws its negatives over everything below it (the D-pack rule: data-v2 hard negatives over all training labels, the data 1.2 in-label-set negatives, false-negative pairs over the union); earlier rows keep their lists, so P0's groups never see a lever label.
    - (4a) The D pack. The 363 synthetic banking, fintech and insurance pack labels of model D's pack (entry 2026-10-07 14:39, re-run note 14:59, result 15:46; manifests 780483b769077ed563461fb1e77467a33d50722d5d6c255e5e0474692451b7dc and 47966714226434e26661798d136069454d80bf8d4a7e4172ed8ec0f8b735a9b0; 9 seed service names written for this project; generator Qwen2.5-14B-Instruct cf98f3b3bbb457ad9e2bb7baf9a0125b6b88caa8, Apache-2.0) with their 4,882 English rows and their lt rows (2,173 translated, 324 native) and pl rows (2,165 translated, 323 native). MultiDoGO is not taken a second time (it is in data pub, E1 (h)). Ingested in job L1 ((14)): E4's removal on every pack row (the MASSIVE test of every locale and the cross-lingual pairs, beyond the D-pack audit already applied), dev wins against data pub's DEV, negatives recomputed by the D-pack rule over data pub and L1's labels (the stored D-pack lists name DSTC11 and ABCD labels that P does not have), R_SYN 10 rows per label per epoch, v2 label texts. Removal counts per rule go into L1's manifest. The 9 seeds were shown in the working chat only after the pack was built: the summary of 2026-10-07 20:49 said that they were in the summary above, which they were not, and they were listed in the working chat at 21:01, in translation, after a question about the item; they are also in `docs/work/stage12/LOOK_BATCH_ru.md` section 3. Their use in P_full was approved at 21:09 ((13)).
    - (4b) V-37 (as registered in E1 and E2): the 37 domains of the frozen domain list (sha256 f6ca6e11b487edf87569b3c33213730fd86952bfa9fa7a18b185b6df109c4db5) that data v2 did not generate (the capped_domains of the data v2 manifest a71da5c9...), generated in job L1 by the GEN_37 = 1 path of the data 1.2 notebook with the unchanged data v2 prompts and generator (Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct a09a35458c702b33eeacc393d103063234e8bc28), budget 120 minutes, DENSE_37 0, SHORT_CMD_SHARE 0.0; 5 domains drawn as fresh held-out DEV pools by the seeded rule before generation, with the added condition of (7); the others (32 when 5 are drawn) are training domains at R_SYN 10. Descriptions, judge and v3s texts by the data 1.2 procedures; lt and pl rows by the ML 1.2 rules; E4's V-37 pair (the data v2 rules, the 0.85 test-name filter and the removal). The fresh pools are English, flagged fresh, and join no NW-P group. If fewer than 5 domains can be drawn, all that can be drawn are drawn (counted).
    - (4c) V-IND (industry packs), the checked draft with all its fixes, adapted to P_full.
      - Industries: 12, written for this project (disclosed), naming service types only: "telecom operator", "energy supplier", "electricity grid operator", "water and heating utility", "online shop", "courier company", "freight forwarder", "property management company", "tax and social security office", "car leasing company", "wholesale supplier", "school administration office". json.dumps of this list (as PACK_SEEDS_SHA in `tools/dpack_build/src/cells/c08_pack_gen.py`) has sha256 76f3f7aad87a605aed6befffac5b65b2d9dffeb4609bf53ab42686d91f5b9d9f. The choice is a judgment, not a measurement: customer-service sectors outside banking and insurance with contract, billing and service intents, some of them business or public-service cases. Public intent data exist for some: the Bitext telco and retail e-commerce verticals (in data 1.2), ABCD for online retail (the information pool abcd_dev) and MultiDoGO, whose paper (Peskov et al., EMNLP 2019, section 3) describes its media domain as customers ordering telecommunications services and paying their bills. The list was shown in translation in the working chat at 20:49; it and the money-side content below were approved at 21:09 ((13)).
      - Overlap, by name only (no cosine was computed for this entry): data 1.2 has synthetic training domains near several industries (electricity supplier, water supply company, mobile phone carrier, parcel delivery, passport office, city council services, university admissions office, second hand marketplace) and the real Bitext telco, retail ecommerce and customer support verticals. On DEV, tax and social security office is near the work domain of clinc_dev (taxes, w2, income, payday, direct deposit), car leasing company near its travel domain (car rental) and the CLINC150 auto and commute training domain, online shop near its home domain (order, order status), abcd_dev, the bitext_dev retail ecommerce labels and the pools synth_dev_unseen_online_auction_site and synth_dev_unseen_bookstore, telecom operator near the bitext_dev telco labels. These overlaps can raise P_full on seen pools; they are guarded by dev wins, by the clinc_dev rule below and by domain_seen_by_P ((7)).
      - Excluded sectors: banking, cards, payment services, lending, fintech and insurance (already covered by Banking77, CLINC banking and credit_cards, the Bitext dom verticals, NLU++ banking, Multi3NLU++ es, MultiDoGO and the D pack; Banking77, MInDS-14 and CLINC banking are registered tests); travel, airlines, public transport, taxis and car rental (MASSIVE and HWU64 transport, SIB-200 travel, clinc_dev travel); food delivery and restaurants (MASSIVE and HWU64 takeaway, the xSID BookRestaurant intent, clinc_dev kitchen and dining); hotels (nlupp_hotels_dev, mixed_dev); media, streaming, music, games, events and ticketing (MASSIVE and HWU64 play, music, audio and recommendation, MTOP event, xSID music, book and screening intents, SIB-200 entertainment and sports, the hint3_dev fantasy sports domain); smart home, alarms, reminders, calendar and e-mail assistants (HWU64, MASSIVE, MTOP, xSID); health care (SIB-200 health, the dental practice pool, eight health-care domains of the frozen list); HR and internal IT helpdesks (clinc_dev work, the checkpoint-selection pool, and the vpn service pool). On 2026-10-06 at 15:44 a reply had named travel and medical appointments among the sectors of possible industry packs (working chat); both are excluded here for the reasons above. To the question of 21:02 why they cannot be taken, the reply was that MASSIVE and HWU64 already cover travel and transport, food delivery, music and media and the smart home with human rows, that health care (the dental practice pool), hotels and office work (HR and IT) are DEV pools on which the not-worse rule checks domains the model has not seen, so that training on these sectors would blind that check, and that a later public model may add these sectors with new DEV pools; the exclusion was approved at 21:09 ((13)). More precisely, nlupp_hotels_dev, mixed_dev and clinc_dev, whose work domain covers HR, are already in domain_seen_by_P ((7)), so for hotels and HR the checks at stake are not NW-P's unseen groups but the checkpoint-selection pool and other DEV checks.
      - Test-family check: on 2026-10-07 a check on label names only (from the frozen description files) found that no seed equals, shares its word set with, or contains a label name of Banking77, HWU64, MASSIVE, xSID, MTOP, MInDS-14, SIB-200 or CLINC banking and credit_cards. The build repeats the D-pack seed and prompt check on all registered intent sets and stops on a hit.
      - Content: the D-pack prompts (the data-v2 object and intent prompts without their finance exclusion sentence) and no data-v2 finance blocklist, so the packs carry billing, payment, refund, deposit, fee and contract intents of non-bank sectors. Data 1.2 applied those blocklists to its synthetic domains and its real sources, so outside banking P0's honest part has no payment, refund, fee or deposit intents and only a few Bitext invoice and shipping-cost intents.
      - Packs, with the D-pack settings unchanged: 8 objects per industry and 6 intents per object, at least 2 about something that went wrong; 3 sampled calls of 6 messages per intent (temperature 0.9, top_p 0.95, top_k 0, seeded); DPACK_DENSE 0, SHORT_CMD_SHARE 0.0; the judge with up to 6 siblings and the 2 nearest other-object intents, acceptance at least 0.35 and at least 6 kept messages, at most 18 kept messages per label; the D-pack deduplication, synonym mask and merge rules with their stops (more than 15% masked or more than 1% merged); a name in more than 3 synthetic domains (base domains count) is dropped; an industry with fewer than 20 intents left is dropped. Generator PACK_GENERATOR auto: Qwen2.5-14B-Instruct (cf98f3b3..., Apache-2.0, its LICENSE file at that revision read on 2026-10-07) in fp16 over two GPUs if its numerics probe passes, else Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct (a09a3545..., Apache-2.0). Probe: the object prompts of all 12 industries and the intent prompts of the first four industries with the objects "mobile plan", "electricity contract", "grid connection" and "water meter"; one forward pass with all logits finite and at least 14 of the 16 greedy outputs parsing into at least half of the requested lines; probe outputs are not used. With fewer than 2 GPUs or under 60 GiB in all, the 7B is used without a probe. The judge, the v2 descriptions by the frozen step-0 procedure, the description check, the re-judge and the v3s texts use Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct. Style references only from the data v2 style-pool sources (Bitext 8 verticals, CLINC150 training domains, MultiWOZ 2.2, HINT3), from domains with name cosine below 0.80 to the industry, with the D-pack filters; never from a split of a registered test family, PRESTO, NLU++, Multi3NLU++, MultiDoGO or the Bitext dom verticals. Pack labels: R_SYN 10, v2 label texts. By the D-pack yield (363 labels from 9 seeds, about 40 per seed), about 320 to 480 training labels are expected from 8 to 12 training industries; an estimate, not a gate.
      - lt and pl rows of the training labels: EuroLLM-9B-Instruct-2512 (def82454026b0353f12e48a0de081a9ed380d91e, Apache-2.0, its README at that revision read on 2026-10-07), 6 rows per label and language; label names by EuroLLM, full label texts by OPUS-MT at the ML 1.2 pins; 3 native rows per label with the D12-3 filter, the native share of each language capped at 0.13 over the union with the base. No other language is added.
      - Fresh held-out industries, drawn before any generation (CPU, names only): eligible are the industries whose bge-m3 (BAAI/bge-m3 5617a9f61b028005a4858fdac845db406aefb181) name cosine is below 0.80 to every other industry of the list, to every name of the frozen domain list (112 entries, so every V-37 domain counts), to every domain of a data pub training source and to the domain of every honest DEV pool. numpy default_rng(0) permutes the sorted eligible list and the first 4 are held out; with 2 or 3 eligible, all of them are held out; there is no fallback, so a held-out industry is never close by name to a training industry. With fewer than 2 eligible, no industry is held out, every industry trains, and V-IND's target is not reported (a change from the draft, which then dropped V-IND: the requests of the working chat require the packs in P). The fresh industries are generated by Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct with the same pack prompts, settings, prompt check, name rules and judge, each as the pool ind_dev_unseen_<slug>, flagged fresh. Train wins against every training label and row of data pub, L1 and V-IND (a fresh label with the same name or name cosine >= 0.90 leaves DEV; a fresh row equal after normalisation or at cosine >= 0.95 leaves DEV). Near-test rows are flagged as for every DEV row. lt and pl copies by OPUS-MT, 8 rows per label, with the ML DEV leakage audit; no de or sk copies. Inside each training industry 5% of the intents (seeded, SYNTH_DEV_INTENT_FRAC 0.05) form the English information pool ind_dev_seen_industry, flagged in the manifest like the fresh pools so that it joins no NW-P group (it is information only, (10a)).
      - Name, DEV and leakage rules: the D-pack name rules (exact name, same word set, a contained registered label name of two or more words, the data-v2 proper-subset rule for intent names, bge-m3 name cosine >= 0.85) against the label names of Banking77, HWU64, MASSIVE, xSID, MTOP, MInDS-14, CLINC banking and credit_cards and the seven SIB-200 topic names, for the packs and the fresh pools alike; every generation prompt is checked as in data v2 and a seed hit stops the build. Dev wins against every DEV pool label of data pub and L1 (same name or name cosine >= 0.90); for clinc_dev, the checkpoint-selection pool, a pack intent with bge-m3 name cosine >= 0.80 to a clinc_dev label is also dropped (TRAIN_DEV_NAME_FLAG_COS); a training row equal after normalisation or at cosine >= 0.95 to a DEV row or to a carve-out row of E2 (5) is dropped. Leakage: the union of the D-pack rule (every new English row at exact match or bge-m3 cosine >= 0.90 to any English text of any split of every registered test family, the confirmatory CLINC rows or the D-pack non-English audit texts is removed; Banking77 train counted on its own and its hits removed) and E4 (the MASSIVE test of every locale, cross-lingual pairs at the threshold of 2026-10-05 03:35, a hit drops the whole translation group), the quoted-example rule at 0.95; test texts read from the pub raw-file store and written only to files whose names hold 'test'; counts per rule, source and audited set in the manifest and, if P_full ships, in the model card appendix.
      - Stops. Quality stops: fewer than 150 training-pack labels after the name filters, after the judge or after every filter; pack judge acceptance below 0.35; a D-pack mask or merge stop. On a quality stop one second attempt runs, after its own short note, registered here: the same seeds, prompts, rules and stops, with 12 objects per industry instead of 8 and the other generator where the PACK_GENERATOR rule allows it (Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct if Qwen2.5-14B-Instruct made the first attempt; Qwen2.5-14B-Instruct if the 7B made it only because fewer than 2 GPUs or under 60 GiB were available; otherwise the same generator); if the second attempt also stops, V-IND is left out of P_full and this is reported in the working chat. Technical stops (a build on fewer than 2 GPUs, which is PROVISIONAL; a build without the look gate; a crash or a missing output) follow (2). Fewer than 2 fresh pools, or fewer than 100 English or 100 lt and pl fresh decision rows, only make the V-IND target 'not measurable' in (10).
    - (4d) E-mail wrappers (V-E's change), the checked part.
      - Share: 0.30 of the eligible cross-encoder groups in en and de and 0.20 in lt and pl (FINAL pub setting AUG_EMAIL_P with its lt and pl value, default 0, refused outside FINAL pub; the lt and pl value is set by the floor of (5)). Eligible: message language en, lt, pl or de, whatever the source, and a gold label that is not a courtesy label (English name, lower case, '_' and '-' read as spaces, containing greet, hello, goodbye, thank or farewell, or the word bye). Reasons for 0.30 (lowered to 0.20 in lt and pl only by the floor of (5)): on the v1.1 model the probe's fixed e-mail wrapper lowered DEV decision-row accuracy by 4.63 points on EN5, 7.80 on LT, 5.26 on PL, 6.55 on LTPL and 4.49 on DE, and over all pools by 5.95 on EN13, 10.79 on LT_ALL, 11.05 on PL_ALL and 7.66 on DE_ALL (`results/noise_probe_v11/noise_probe.md` lines 131 to 141; entry 2026-10-06 17:50); the registered B-7 rule sets 0.30 for a measured loss of at least 3 points with its interval excluding 0, and every e-mail loss above is larger; published casing augmentation at 50% kept clean intent accuracy within 0.7 points (Sengupta et al. 2021, Table 5) and a typo in 50% of the training queries of a BERT re-ranker left clean effectiveness not significantly different (Zhuang and Zuccon 2021); a wrapper changes no word of the message.
      - Wrappers: 200 per language (en, lt, pl, de), each a pair (greeting part, closing part) generated together. A language that keeps 100 to 199 after the third round uses all of them; a language that keeps fewer than 100 gets no wrappers and its groups are not wrapped; only if all four keep fewer than 100 is the lever left out (a change from the draft, which then dropped V-E: the requests of the working chat require the e-mail lever in P).
      - Generator: utter-project/EuroLLM-9B-Instruct-2512 at def82454026b0353f12e48a0de081a9ed380d91e (Apache-2.0) for all four languages, replacing Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct in E1 and E2 ((13)). Reason: the Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct card claims over 29 languages and names 13 of them, Lithuanian and Polish not among them, and the Qwen2 language list names Polish and German but not Lithuanian; the EuroLLM card lists English, Lithuanian, Polish and German, and the model is pinned (`hpc/prefetch.py` lines 38 to 43) and made ML 1.2's lt and pl rows (manifest 9ece4703..., entry 2026-10-06 23:08). No number was used for this choice. Loading and decoding as ML 1.2 (`llm_load`, `llm_generate` of `notebooks/step1_build_multilingual_v3.ipynb`): fp16, never bf16, the job stops if the fp16 logits of the first prompts are not finite; on one V100 (32 GB; the 9B model in fp16 takes about 18 GB, so ML 1.2's split over two GPUs, which leaves one GPU idle at a time, is not needed); an explicit generation configuration; the chat template with a system turn; left padding; length-sorted batches of 32, halved on out-of-memory; ML 1.2's native sampling (temperature 0.8, top_p 0.95, top_k 0, repetition penalty 1.0); max_new_tokens 1,024. The cache key of a call is the sha1 of the JSON list [model, revision, 've-v1', language, round, slot, call, prompt file sha256, sampling settings, max_new_tokens]; before each batch torch is seeded with the first 8 hex digits, read as an integer, of the sha1 of the key of the batch's first call. Outputs are reproducible from the cache, not by rerunning the model.
      - Prompt: `docs/work/stage12/variants/ve_wrapper_prompt_v1.txt`, written in English for this project, sha256 90d3e5a3b349692cda7015b7d918d8fabdbdc8ffb2843994865e11bb5b9d76a2 (UTF-8 bytes of the whole file, 3,813 bytes), committed with this entry; generation refuses another sha256. Each call asks for five frames, each of five labelled lines (SALUTATION, OPENING, CLOSING, SIGNOFF, NAME), under one of 81 slots used in turn (register formal, neutral or informal x 0, 1 or 2 opening sentences x 0, 1 or 2 closing sentences x first name, full name or none). It forbids any reference to the body or to anything specific and any digit, link, contact detail, placeholder, bracket, quotation mark, emoji, markdown, numbering or subject line, and contains no greeting, closing, sign-off or name in any language and no example text.
      - Job: after the look, data pub and ML pub, a SLURM job of its own (one V100, `--time 03:00:00`) with the look gate. Round 1: 243 calls per language (3 per slot); rounds 2 and 3 only for a language with fewer than 200 kept wrappers; at most 3,645 raw wrappers per language. A job that stops before the wrapper file is written is a technical stop of (2): a code-only fix, then one rerun with the same prompt, settings and cache; if the rerun also stops, the lever is left out and this is reported in the working chat.
      - Filters, in this order, counted per language and rule: (a) parse (ML 1.2's `no_dashes`, five labelled lines per frame, at most the first 5 frames per call, the last frame of an output cut at max_new_tokens dropped, slot and none mismatches dropped, NAME at most 3 words); (b) length: each part 1 to 30 words, both together at most 60; (c) form: no digit, no '@', 'http' or 'www', none of [ ] { } < > _ | # * :, no Unicode symbol character, no repeated n-grams; (d) language: lower-cased letters only from the language's alphabet (lt adds ą č ę ė į š ų ū ž; pl adds ą ć ę ł ń ó ś ź ż; de adds ä ö ü ß), for lt, pl and de also ML 1.2's `native_reason` check, and an lt, pl or de wrapper of at least 8 words must contain a letter of its added set; (e) no intent: (i) after lower-casing and `strip_diacritics` (name line excepted), the whole English name of a non-courtesy data pub label that has a word of at least 5 letters, or two different such words of one name (a word counts when equal, or when both have at least 6 letters and the same first 6), rejects the wrapper; single common courtesy words stay allowed; (ii) a part or the whole wrapper at bge-m3 cosine >= 0.80 to a non-courtesy English label name of data pub is rejected; (f) noise probe (`tools/noise_probe_build/src/r2intent_noise.py` sha256 ae08787930937a7c3bed33babd2d3a1d9e1d0a27c83bfbf3b1a7b7702893c9e6, EMAIL_TEMPLATES lines 26 to 47): after `norm_text_ml`, a part that equals or contains a probe part, contains a probe line or sentence of at least 4 words or a probe signature name, or has bge-m3 cosine >= 0.90 to the probe part of the same role in any language, is rejected; formulas of at most 3 words stay allowed (disclosed); (g) a part sharing a run of 5 words with the prompt file is rejected; (h) duplicates: exact (whole wrapper after `norm_text_ml`), then bge-m3 cosine >= 0.93 to a kept one, in generation order; (i) leakage and DEV with the audit code of data pub and ML pub: a wrapper whose greeting part, closing part or whole text matches a message of any audited set of E4 in any language exactly (`norm_text_ml` and `fold_ml`) or at bge-m3 cosine >= 0.90 is removed (counts only; test texts only in files whose names hold 'test'), and so is one at cosine >= 0.95 to a DEV message of data pub or of its ML pub copies, every pool and language copy included.
      - Choice: kept wrappers of a language sorted by sha256 of 've-v1|<lang>|<greeting part>|<closing part>', the first 200 chosen. The wrapper file, the raw cache, the filled prompts, the seeds and the counts are written once, and the wrapper file's sha256 is recorded before the P job. Optional reading by the person who gave the delegation (not a gate): before the P job is built the chosen lt (and pl) wrappers may be read, and any that is not natural, correct text or says more than courtesy may be struck; a struck wrapper is replaced by the next kept one in sha256 order (not read again); strike ids go into a file whose sha256 is recorded with the wrapper file's; the counts are in the record and the model card; without a strike file the chosen wrappers are used and the record says no reading was done.
      - Use at sampling time in the cross-encoder (FINAL pub P_full only; the adapter by (4g)): stream [seed, epoch, 404] gives u1 and u2 for every group of the epoch plan in plan order, drawn only when AUG_EMAIL_P is above 0, so P0's plans are unchanged. An eligible group is wrapped if u1 is below its language's share, with wrapper floor(u2 x N) of its language's list, as greeting part, blank line, message, blank line, closing part (`r2intent_noise.email_wrap` layout). The message is the group's message after the language view and the other changes of (5); a stripped message gets a stripped wrapper. A drawn group is not wrapped (counted) if its greeting part, a blank line and its message exceed 78 tokens of the bge-reranker-v2-m3 tokenizer, so no message token is cut (PAIR_MAX_LEN 160, 4 special tokens, longest_first). Gold label, negatives and label texts are those of the unwrapped group; the adapter's messages are wrapped by their own draws ((4g)). The record lists per epoch and language the eligible, drawn, wrapped and length-skipped groups, courtesy exclusions, the realised share and the use count of each wrapper.
    - (4e) V-TYPO (as registered): AUG_TYPO_P 0.10 (the registered share, in lt and pl too: no factor, (5)), one rule-based typo by the typing-error rule of the entry of 2026-10-06 17:18 in the en, lt and pl cross-encoder draws, drawn from the new stream [seed, epoch, 405] (patch P asserts it is unused elsewhere); in P_full's adapter one typo at the same share by (4g), which amends E2's 'none in the adapter' ((13)); made at sampling time from audited rows, so no text is stored.
    - (4f) V-MIX (as registered): LABEL_MODE_MIX True in FINAL pub for P_full only; honest English groups (the E1 (a) rows and V-37's rows, made by the same generator and procedures) draw v2 0.40, v3s-A 0.125, v3s-B 0.125, noex 0.20, name + first sentence 0.05, name 0.10; closed-inventory groups draw their frozen inference text 60% and that mix 40%; every other group keeps v2 or its text by E2 (3). The mode is drawn from the existing stream [seed, epoch, 303] of the stage-1.2 sampler. V-MIX is the first lever lowered in a repair ((9)).
    - (4g) V-ADIA (the NOISE-PUB draft for stripping; wrappers and typos added by the answer to item 10 of (13)): P_full's retrieval adapter (E2 (6), B's ONE-0 recipe, AD_STEPS rule unchanged) also trains on noisy messages, with the three changes of P_full's cross-encoder groups at the same shares ((5)): diacritic stripping (lt and pl, AUG_STRIP_AD_LTPL 0.30), one e-mail wrapper (en and de 0.30, lt and pl 0.20) and one typo (en, lt and pl, 0.10). For each drawn adapter message each change is drawn independently from its own new stream, which patch P asserts is unused elsewhere: stripping [seed, 203] (one uniform number), wrapper [seed, 204] (two uniform numbers u1 and u2, as in (4d)) and typo [seed, 205] (the draws of the typing-error rule). A message's language is its message language whatever the source (translated, native, a MASSIVE pl-PL or de-DE row or a PRESTO row), as in (5), and the changes are applied in the order stripping, typo, wrapper: an lt or pl message is replaced by `r2intent_v11_lib.strip_diacritics` of itself when its stripping number is below 0.30; an en, lt or pl message gets one rule-based typo by the typing-error rule of the entry of 2026-10-06 17:18 when its typo number is below 0.10; a message eligible by the rule of (4d) (language en, lt, pl or de with wrappers, gold label not a courtesy label) is wrapped when u1 is below its language's share, with wrapper floor(u2 x N) of its language's list of (4d) in the layout of (4d), a stripped message getting a stripped wrapper. A drawn message is not wrapped (counted) if its greeting part, a blank line and its message exceed 126 tokens of the bge-reranker-v2-m3 tokenizer, because `train_adapter` truncates a message at MSG_MAX_LEN 128 tokens with 2 special tokens; so no message token is cut. The MSE target of a changed message stays the vector that its clean message (unstripped, untyped, unwrapped) uses under E2 (6) and `adapter_items` (`tools/step1_stage12_build/src/r2intent_v11_lib.py` line 1881): the frozen bge-m3 vector of the English source row for a translation, of the en-US parallel row for a MASSIVE locale row, and of the message itself for an English or other native row; gold label, label texts and the order stream [seed, 202] are unchanged. Reason: the cross-encoder changes of (4d), (4e) and (5) do not reach retrieval, while on the LTPL rows the probe's retrieval recall at 5 fell from 0.943 to 0.909 with diacritics removed, to 0.891 under the e-mail wrapper and to 0.916 under one typo, and on EN5 from 0.980 to 0.959 under the e-mail wrapper (`results/noise_probe_v11/noise_probe.md` lines 55, 134, 28 and 131; entry 2026-10-06 17:50); a message whose gold label leaves the top 5 cannot be recovered by any cross-encoder change, and the fused score (cross logit plus 60 times the adapter cosine) also depends on the adapter for the rows that stay in the top 5. The shares are those of P_full's cross-encoder ((5); the stripping share 0.30 was set by T-DIA under a rule logged before its DEV number); no further DEV number chose them. The changed messages are made at sampling time from rows that passed E4 and from the audited wrappers of (4d), and no row is stored. The record lists per language the drawn, stripped, typo, wrapped and length-skipped adapter messages and the realised clean share. If a WiSE mix ships, Z's part of the rank-64 concatenation stays Z's adapter. P0's adapter gets no changed message (E2 (4)). Stripping in the adapter was delegated decision 5 (the V-ADIA draft); the wrapper and typo parts replace that decision's exclusion of them, by the answer to item 10.
  - (5) Message changes and the clean floor. In P_full each change is drawn independently per cross-encoder group from its own stream: diacritic stripping (lt and pl; the plan's ML_STRIP_P draw, stream [seed, epoch, 101] of the ML plan), the e-mail wrapper (en, lt, pl, de; [seed, epoch, 404]) and one typo (en, lt, pl; [seed, epoch, 405]). A group is clean when its message has none of them; the label-text mix of V-MIX is a label-side change outside this rule. The registered shares (stripping 0.30, wrapper 0.30, typo 0.10) leave clean shares of 0.63 for en, 0.70 for de and 0.441 for lt and pl. Rule: per language at least 0.50 of the groups stay clean at the nominal shares. For lt and pl the wrapper share is lowered to 0.20, and stripping and typo keep their registered shares 0.30 and 0.10: clean share 0.7 x 0.9 x 0.8 = 0.504. en and de keep the registered shares. No common factor is used. This is the answer to item 9 ((13)): it replaces delegated decision 5, which multiplied every lt and pl share by one common factor of 0.866 (stripping 0.2598, wrapper 0.2598, typo 0.0866, clean share 0.5004), and it keeps the stripping share of the item named at 18:45 ('text without Lithuanian letters') at the registered 0.30 in P_full, as in P0. When several changes hit one group they are applied in the order language view, stripping, typo, wrapper. The realised clean share per language and epoch is recorded. P0 keeps AUG_STRIP_P_LTPL 0.30 and no other change. A language of a group is its message language whatever the source, so MASSIVE pl-PL and de-DE rows and PRESTO de and en rows take their language's changes, in P0 (stripping) and P_full alike; patch P applies the plan's stripping draw to every lt and pl group, not only to the groups of the ML view, which widens E2 (4)'s 'set as ML_STRIP_P 0.30' for P0 as well (declared in (13)). The teacher of (6) always scores the clean message. P_full's adapter draws the same three changes at the same shares from its own streams ((4g)), so its clean share per language is the same.
  - (6) V-TEACH: the teacher probe and the KD term (the checked V-TEACH part, reduced to two candidates after the statement of 19:08 in the working chat).
    - Candidates (licences read at the primary source on 2026-10-07; each card and licence file is read again at download and its sha256 recorded): Qwen/Qwen3-Reranker-8B at 77d193c791ed757ca307ee72715aa132723da912 (the pin of `hpc/prefetch.py` line 60; 8.2B parameters by the HF safetensors metadata at the pin, 8B on the card), Apache-2.0 by the front matter of its model card and by the Qwen3 Embedding report (arXiv 2506.05176, "the Qwen3 Embedding models are publicly available under the Apache 2.0 license"); the repository has no LICENSE file; the card claims over 100 languages and names none; its prose calls the series 'the latest proprietary model of the Qwen family', while its front matter and arXiv 2506.05176 state Apache-2.0, which this entry follows. google/gemma-4-31B-it at 842da3794eaa0b77d5f08bae87a17459d91ff475 (30.7B parameters by its card), Apache-2.0: the card names apache-2.0 with the licence link https://ai.google.dev/gemma/docs/gemma_4_license, which is the Apache 2.0 text (last updated 2026-04-01); the Gemma Terms of Use (modified 2026-04-01) refer Gemma 4 to that licence, so their Model Derivatives clause, which covers distillation, does not reach Gemma 4; the Prohibited Use Policy is linked only from the page's navigation; the card states out-of-the-box support for 35+ languages and pre-training on 140+, and names neither Lithuanian nor Polish. Apache-2.0 sets no condition on the use of a model's outputs, so by the licence texts (not a legal opinion) training P on these scores and licensing P under CC BY-NC 4.0 or commercially is allowed. Section 6 grants no trademark rights: P's name contains no Qwen or Gemma name, and the card and NOTICE name the teacher, its revision and its licence without implying endorsement. No teacher weight is distributed. Who chose them: Gemma 4 31B is mandatory by the request of 19:08 (two teachers are enough, Gemma 4 31B necessarily, as the best one), written in reply to the proposal of 19:07 to probe four teachers and then a 122B stage; Qwen3-Reranker-8B as the second teacher is the recommendation in the reply of 18:42, restated at 19:08 and confirmed by the 'yes' of 19:08 without objection; leaving out Qwen3-Reranker-4B, Qwen3.5-27B, Qwen3.5-122B-A10B (no stage B) and every other model of the teacher research of 2026-10-07 follows from the words 'two are enough'. Gemma 4 31B is always probed.
    - Reading of E2 (amended in (13)): 'model, revision, rows and loss weight named in a note under this entry before J7' is met by naming the two pinned candidates and the fixed DEV rule below; no other model can be the teacher, and the rule picks at most one. 'Used only in training' means never at inference, never shipped and never evaluated on a test set; apart from training, the teacher's only use is the DEV probe, which serves only this choice. Only the teacher's distributions over candidates enter the loss; no new text enters training; the shipped P keeps its size and its 40 ms T4 rule. The listwise prompt is written for this project and is not training text.
    - Engines. Reranker: HF transformers in fp16, one V100 per replica, the transformers default attention (SDPA; flash-attention does not run on sm70), through a copy of the look's Qwen3 reranker code path (class Q3Reranker, `tools/step2_onefile_build/src/r2intent_onefile_lib.py` lines 1180 to 1227), the card template, the fixed instruction RR_TASK of the look's Qwen3 baseline (line 1073), at most 1,024 tokens per pair (line 1197), score = logit(yes) minus logit(no) at the last position. Gemma 4 31B, primary route: llama.cpp built with CUDA 12.8 for sm70 (CMAKE_CUDA_ARCHITECTURES 70; builds with CUDA 12.9 fail weight-buffer allocation on V100, llama.cpp issue 28416); a Q8_0 GGUF of the text model made on the cluster in a CPU job from the pinned safetensors by that commit's convert and quantize tools, sha256 recorded; one replica over two V100s (the Q8_0 file is about 33 GB); the letter logits at the answer position are read through llama-cpp-python built from the pinned commit (its low-level API) or through a small program on that commit's libllama, never through llama-server's n_probs, which returns only the top tokens. The llama.cpp commit and its build and run flags (context, batch sizes, parallel slots, flash-attention setting) are implementation pins chosen after J7, in the GPU smoke, on stand-in prompts and without any DEV score; they are logged in the entry of the job that first uses them, bind only through the reference checks of the Failures bullet, and no accuracy number may enter their choice. Prerequisites, confirmed before this entry is logged and exercised in the GPU smoke: a CUDA 12.8 nvcc and cuBLAS on the cluster (a module, the pip nvidia-cuda-nvcc and cuBLAS wheels, or a CUDA 12.8 devel image; the cluster environment is pip torch 2.11.0+cu126 on driver 550.144.03, `results/final_dom_245587/hpc_job_info.txt`, and `hpc/run_notebook.sbatch` removes CUDA folders from LD_LIBRARY_PATH); google/gemma-4-31B-it at the pin in the prefetch, with a disk check (about 62 GB of safetensors, an intermediate GGUF and the 33 GB Q8_0); access to the CPU partition main. Fallback route, registered now and used only if the primary route fails in the GPU smoke (stand-in prompts, before any DEV row is scored): HF transformers in fp16, text-only, over 3 V100s of one node (about 61 GB of weights; found feasible by the teacher research of 2026-10-07), with the same template, letters, orders, reference and checks; its A2 holds three GPUs, so its GPU-hours are about 1.5 times the primary route's at equal speed. If the cluster grants no node with 3 V100s, there is no fallback route and the smoke failure is a technical stop of (2). Checked on 2026-10-07 at about 22:10 (read only): the login node has no CUDA module and no nvcc on its path; the pip wheels nvidia-cuda-nvcc-cu12 12.8.61 and 12.8.93 are listed by the venv's pip index, so the 12.8 nvcc comes from that wheel in a separate build environment, and a binary built for sm_70 with CUDA 12.8 runs on driver 550 under CUDA 12 minor-version compatibility (no PTX JIT is needed for sm_70 code), which the GPU smoke confirms; each gpu node has 8 V100-SXM2-32GB (sinfo: node B, node C, node D), so a 3-V100 allocation exists; the CPU partition main has no time limit, 48 cores and about 376 GB of memory per node, and job 245588 ran there; the scratch file system shows no user or group quota and about 166 TB free (lfs quota, df).
    - Prompt (Gemma): one prompt per list; its user turn is `docs/work/stage12/vteach/vteach_listwise_prompt_v1.txt` (sha256 d237e0cd505859945ec28a0d9bec078f3a0890f37404cb9cb3bfba2e1574957f, committed with this entry) with {message} and {options}, one line per candidate (letter, ') ', the candidate's v2 label text), letters A to E in the probe and A to H in training; rendered by the model's own chat template with the generation prompt and thinking off (enable_thinking False, so no think token and the template's empty thought channel), never built by hand. A candidate's log-probability is the log softmax, in fp32, over the logits of the list's letter tokens only, read at the position after the rendered generation prompt. Each list is scored in two fixed orders, a seeded shuffle (stream [0, 506] in the probe, [seed, 507] in training; patch P asserts both are unused) and its reverse, never Z's rank order or the plan's slot order; the mean of the two log-probabilities is the candidate's score. p_teacher is the softmax of the teacher's own scores at T = 1, not tuned; its sharpness depends on the score scale of each teacher (disclosed).
    - Probe rows: the decision rows of P's DEV that enter EN_DEC (0.5 REAL + 0.5 PIPE, without the manifest's pub_domain_pools) and LTPL, as NW-P defines them. With pub_domain_pools equal to dstc11_insurance alone these are 2,889 English rows (REAL 311: hint3_dev 121, sgd_unseen 190; PIPE 2,578) and 4,245 lt and pl rows (lt 2,150, pl 2,095), counted in `results/stage12_245443/stage12_decision_20261006T204627Z.json` (rule_group_rows and b_reproduction.g5.pools): 7,134 lists, 35,670 reranker pairs and 14,268 Gemma prompts; the realised counts on data pub are recorded. Each list is Z's retrieval top 5 at k 5 with the message and the v2 label texts that Z's cross-encoder reads. Each candidate is used alone. A candidate's accuracy is the share of rows whose top-scored candidate is the gold label; a row whose highest score is shared by two or more candidates counts as a miss; its EN_DEC, LTPL and S are computed from these rows exactly as Z's.
    - Z gate: Z (configs.B) is re-scored in job A1 at w 60, k 5 and must reproduce its stored stage-1.2 DEV frame (`stage12_dev_scores_B_20261006T204627Z.parquet`, sha256 3d137c5fab66181f5eb126c1d3d1af34c8b1db68e298665adfa5079a466c09af, registered in the model D entry of 2026-10-07 16:00) within 0.003 accuracy per language and pool group on the matched decision rows (the G5 tolerance of the entry of 2026-10-06 23:44). S = 0.5 EN_DEC + 0.5 LTPL; S(Z) is Z's fused value at w 60, k 5 on the probe rows.
    - Jobs, after data pub and ML pub and before any P training. A GPU smoke on the cluster (two V100s, `--time 01:00:00`) loads both candidates and the llama.cpp build on generated stand-in prompts (no DEV row) and measures Gemma's prefill speed on stand-in prompts of the probe's length (330 to 420 tokens) with the pinned slot and batch flags; a load problem may be fixed only in the build or the package versions, named in A1's entry. A1 (two V100s, `--time 02:00:00`): Z's re-scoring and gate, the probe list file (with Z's top 5 on the first 300 clinc_dev decision rows for the checks) written once with its sha256, then the reranker as two replicas, one per GPU, with its checks. A1 is FINAL when Z's gate passed and the list file is written, whatever happens to the reranker: a reranker failure is a candidate failure and does not block A2. A fault of Z's gate that comes from code is a technical stop of (2) (a code-only fix, then one rerun of A1 after its own note). A2 (two V100s; three on the fallback route), after a FINAL A1: Gemma 4 31B on A1's list file, every list in both orders; its --time is 1.5 times the scoring time projected from the smoke's measured speed plus 30 minutes for loading and checks, logged in A2's entry before it runs (about 05:30:00 at the external anchor of (12)). Gemma's reference runs in CPU-only jobs on partition main (HF transformers bf16, `--mem 160G`, `--cpus-per-task 32`, 0 GPU-hours) after A1's list file exists; a 2-prompt timing job on that partition, run after the GPU smoke, sets the reference job's --time (twice the projected time plus 1 hour); if that exceeds the partition's limit, the 200 reference prompts are split over several jobs on disjoint prompts, each logged before it runs. Each job's sbatch line and notebook sha256 (for A2 also the llama.cpp commit, the GGUF sha256 and the transformers version of the reference) are logged in a short entry before it runs, and its guard refuses every test-named file. The probe's code copies the functions it reuses into its own build and does not change the look's sources (`tools/step2_onefile_build/src`) before J7.
    - Failures: a candidate fails if it does not load or runs out of memory after the technical rule of (2) and the fallback route of the Engines bullet are used up; if any probe score is not finite; the reranker: if, on the first 300 clinc_dev decision rows (Z's top 5; the fp32 reference split over both GPUs, `r2intent_onefile_lib.py` lines 1078 and 1096), its fp16 against fp32 top-1 agreement is below 0.99 (the numerics rule that AMENDMENTS_12 T-2 and RC-15 set for the look's Qwen3 baselines, written out here: no NaN or inf, fp16 against fp32 top-1 agreement at least 0.99 on the first 300 clinc_dev rows, and a failure is never rerun in another precision), or its batched top-1 differs from the card's reference snippet on any of the first 4 clinc_dev decision rows (20 pairs; line 1283); Gemma: if on the first 100 clinc_dev decision rows in both orders (200 prompts), against the bf16 CPU reference, any value is not finite, the top-1 agreement is below 0.99 or the mean KL(p_reference || p_engine) is 0.01 nats or more; if a letter is not a single token, the token ids of llama.cpp and the HF tokenizer differ on a reference prompt, or the mean probability on the letters (full-vocabulary softmax at the answer position, by the reference) is below 0.5; if the reference jobs end without a result (one identical rerun of a CPU job is allowed for an infrastructure stop); or if a candidate has not scored every list (Gemma: in both orders) within its job. Near ties: in both agreement checks a top-1 disagreement counts only when the reference's top-two margin is above 0.01 (the reranker: in logit(yes) minus logit(no); Gemma: in the mean log-probability over the two orders); near-tie disagreements are reported separately and do not count. A failed candidate is recorded, its scores are not used, and it is never rerun in another precision, engine, quantisation, template or batch setting other than the fallback route registered above; a technical stop of A1 or A2 before any score of a candidate is read follows (2), one rerun after its own note.
    - Choice of the teacher T: every candidate that did not fail is checked against S(c) >= S(Z) + 0.015; among those that pass, T has the highest unrounded S, as the reply of 19:08 promised ('if there are two such, we take the more accurate one'); an exact tie goes to Gemma 4 31B, the preference stated in the request of 19:08. Values are compared unrounded; a value equal to a threshold passes. The margin: 0.010, the size of VR's gain clause, as a conservative design choice (not a bound: in Rank-DistiLLM, arXiv 2405.07920, the cross-encoder student matched or slightly exceeded its LLM teacher in domain), plus 0.005, B's seed range on the pooled target rounded up (0.0043 over seeds 0, 1 and 2, dev_table of `results/stage12_seeds_245445/stage12_seeds_20261006T232026Z.json`, entry 2026-10-07 11:12; measured on the stage-1.2 EN_DEC with dstc11_insurance, so only a rough floor). Halves: English groups get the KD term only if EN_DEC(T) >= EN_DEC(Z) + 0.004; lt and pl groups only if LTPL(T) >= LTPL(Z) + 0.007 (B's seed ranges 0.0034 and 0.0064 in the same record, rounded up); de groups only if both halves pass. The teacher is not used if: no candidate survives the failures; no candidate passes the margin; Z does not reproduce; a quantity of Z or of a non-failed candidate is NaN; the probe rows differ from P's DEV decision rows; A1 does not end FINAL after the technical rule of (2) (Z's gate failed, a guard refusal or a missing output). The probe is never repeated with other candidates, rows, prompts, instructions or margins. The choice is made once, when A2's result and Gemma's reference check are logged (or when A1's result is logged, if A2 does not run). The probe's result is logged in its own entry; its numbers are DEV information and change nothing else.
    - KD rows: the KD term applies only to groups whose row is an honest row (E1 (a), or its data v2 and ML v2 replacement under (3)) and whose message language is en, lt, pl or de in a half that passed (de: both halves). Every other group keeps CE only, as in P0: the closed inventories (Banking77, HWU64, MASSIVE in every locale, CLINC150 banking and credit_cards), PRESTO, the Bitext dom verticals, NLU++ banking, Multi3NLU++ es, MultiDoGO, the EuroLLM translations of new public rows, the D pack, V-37, V-IND, every other language. Reasons: cross-entropy only, with no distillation, on rows of the closed inventories is the rule of PLAN_80 2.2 item 4 (written out here; that file is not committed); keeping the teacher off the inventories of registered test label sets and off the sources that cover registered test domains limits what an undisclosed teacher training set can pass into P; the probe measured the teacher only on rows of the honest distribution. Because the teacher reads the clean message ((5)), a stripped, typo or wrapped group is eligible and the student learns to match the teacher's clean-text distribution from its noisy input (the checked part excluded stripped groups because the teacher then read the stripped text; that reason no longer holds).
    - Teacher input: the group's clean message (after the language view, before stripping, typo and wrapper) and the English v2 texts of its 8 candidates (`teacher_label_texts` with mode v2, `r2intent_s12_lib.py` line 1011).
    - Scoring cap and job: the scoring of training groups is capped at 8 V100-hours (wall time times the GPUs held; delegated decision 9); the hours guard of (12) may lower the cap, and nothing raises it without an explicit yes in the working chat ((13)). The eligible groups of P_full's epoch 0 and epoch 1 plans at seed 0 are ordered in three tiers, lt and pl first, then en, then de, each tier in a uniform order drawn with the new stream [seed, 505] (asserted unused), and are scored in that order until the cap; groups not scored get CE only, counted per language. The reranker scores each distinct pair of clean message and label text once; Gemma scores each group as one list of 8 in both orders. Expected coverage at the cap if both halves pass (a planning estimate from the speeds of (12), not a measurement): about 3.4 h of scoring time remain on two V100s after the 15-minute stop margin, the plan precompute and loading; the KD-eligible honest groups are about 95,000 over the two epochs (PLAN_80's about 97,000 groups per epoch at an honest share of about 0.49), about 20,000 to 24,000 of them lt or pl (ML_LANG_P 0.125 + 0.125); Gemma, one replica over two GPUs at 497 to 950 prefill tokens per second and about 1,200 to 1,400 prompt tokens per group (8 label texts, two orders), scores about 4,000 to 10,000 groups, a sixth to a half of the lt and pl tier and no English group; Qwen3-Reranker-8B, two replicas at 0.05 to 0.09 s per pair and 8 pairs per group (fewer after the de-duplication of pairs), scores about 34,000 to 61,000 groups, the whole lt and pl tier and about 10,000 to 41,000 English groups. The realised coverage per language and tier is recorded and stated in the model card. The scoring job runs after the lever data jobs and before the P job, on two V100s (`--time 04:00:00`); its loop stops 15 minutes before --time and writes what it has; its sbatch line and notebook sha256 are logged in a short entry before it runs. It writes the false-negative mask EX and P_full's two epoch plans with their sha256, and stores float32 scores per epoch, step and slot with a fingerprint of each plan over rows, negatives, languages, clean messages, the KD mask and the teacher's v2 label texts; the student's label-text modes are not in the fingerprint, because the teacher never reads them, and they and the message changes are drawn in the worker from their own streams. The P_full worker of the P job and every rerun of (9) load the staged EX and plans, check their sha256, recompute them and report any difference, and train on the staged plans; training refuses to start only if a staged file's sha256 or a fingerprint differs. A group with a non-finite teacher score is dropped from the KD term and counted; if more than 0.1% of the scored groups have one (the Translate-Distill rule, `r2intent_s12_lib.py` lines 1350 to 1354), the teacher is not used.
    - Loss: CE + 1.0 x KL(p_teacher || p_student) at T = 1 over the group's 8 candidates; the KL of each KD group is summed and divided by the 16 groups of the step, as `cross_backward_kd` does (`r2intent_s12_lib.py` lines 1144 to 1174; LAMBDA_KD 1.0 and KD_T 1.0, `cells_s12_part1.py` lines 113, 114 and 128). The KD gradient check of the preflight (`cells_s12_part3.py` lines 613 to 636) must pass and must find a KD group in the first 50 steps; if it fails, the job stops before training, the fault is fixed in code only (never in the rule, teacher, rows or weight) and the job runs once more after its own note; if the check fails again, P_full trains without the KD term and the teacher is recorded as not used. The adapter has no KD term.
  - (7) domain_seen_by_P and the draw order.
    - One list for P_full, P0, every WiSE mix and every rerun: {clinc_dev, sgd_unseen, dstc11_insurance, bitext_dev, nlupp_hotels_dev, mixed_dev} plus every pool whose name, or the domain of at least half of its rows, has bge-m3 cosine >= 0.80 to a domain of an E1 source, a MASSIVE or HWU64 scenario, a PRESTO domain, a seed domain of the D pack, one of the V-37 training domains or one of the V-IND training industries, with all language copies. Computed on DEV files and names only, written in the note under E2 before J6b and never changed after it is written (a lever or industry dropped later stays on the list). The fresh pools of V-37 and V-IND and the pool ind_dev_seen_industry ((4c)) join no NW-P group. Disclosure: the union removes rows from the unseen groups of NW-P and so lowers their power; the note before J6b reports the realised row counts and the recomputed false-fail rate (E2).
    - Draw order (names and seeds only, on the CPU, before J6b): first V-IND's eligibility and held-out draw ((4c)); its eligibility already requires cosine below 0.80 to every name of the frozen list, so every V-IND fresh industry is far from every V-37 domain. Then V-37's registered seeded draw, with one added condition: a domain whose bge-m3 name cosine to any V-IND training industry is 0.80 or more is never drawn as fresh (it stays a training domain), and a drawn domain that fails the condition is replaced by the next domain of the same seeded order that meets the registered rule and this condition; the registered rule's fallback never draws such a domain. Both draws, their cosines and the replacements are written in the domain_seen_by_P note.
  - (8) The P job.
    - One SLURM job (FINAL pub with patch P) on two V100s of one node and equal type: GPU 0 trains P_full; GPU 1 first re-scores Z on P's DEV (NW-P needs Z in the same job) and then trains P0, as the two arms of job 245443 were trained. Each arm has its own precompute on GPU 0 before the workers start (the data, ML, EX, E_LT, T_MSG and T_LAB, the selection-pool retrieval, the plans and their fingerprints, and the preflight; GPU 1 waits meanwhile): P0's training files are data pub and ML pub, and its worker refuses lever outputs; P_full's are the union of data pub, L1 and V-IND with their ML parts, and, when a teacher is used, the staged EX and plans of (6). Each worker gets its own DATA and PATHS. The DEV files are the union DEV for both workers and for Z, with the fresh pools of V-37 and V-IND and the pool ind_dev_seen_industry flagged in the manifest so that they join no NW-P group. `--time 10:00:00`; the worker deadlines come from patch P's time plan at the measured speeds, both precomputes included, and are logged with the notebook sha256 and the sbatch line in a short entry before the job runs. Seed 0 for both. AD_STEPS per worker by E2 (6). A worker that does not end FINAL for a technical reason is a technical stop of (2): a code-only fix, then one rerun of that worker on one V100 after its own note (P_full with Z re-scored on the same GPU after training, or Z's re-scoring with P0), before any step of (9) runs; P0 can ship only after P_full, its WiSE mixes of (9) step 2 and any rerun of step 3 (with its mixes) have failed NW-P; the low-a mixes of P_full (step 6) come after P0 and its mixes ((9)).
    - Checkpoint selection, P_full, P0 and every rerun: E2's criterion (the cross-encoder alone over the frozen bge-m3 top 8 on the clinc_dev rows not flagged near_test; evaluations every 400 steps with v1's 12% rule and at the end; ties to the earlier step), restricted to the evaluations whose step is at least the number of steps of epoch 0, and the end-of-training evaluation; epoch 0's evaluations are recorded as information. Reason: caps are per epoch, so a checkpoint at or after the end of epoch 0 has seen every lever's rows at least once; model D's selection stopped at step 1,600 of 5,114, in epoch 0 (entry 2026-10-07 17:56).
    - After training each worker scores its model, and the job scores Z, on P's DEV and VAL and on the targets of (10a); NW-P is computed for P_full and for P0 against Z; both one-file packages are written, and only the shipped one is frozen (9). Every scored model stores its DEV frame with base_row_id (needed by (10b)). The record holds per worker the realised shares per language (stripped, wrapped, length-skipped, typo, clean), the label-mode shares, the KD groups per language and tier, the adapter's stripped, typo, wrapped and length-skipped counts per language with its realised clean share, the plan fingerprints and every selection evaluation.
  - (9) Gate and repair ladder (replaces E2's F1 to F4 for P v1; delegated decision 8, with steps 6 and 7 added by the answer to item 8 of (13)). Each step runs only if the steps before it did not give a shipped model. Every job of the ladder passes the hours guard of (12).
    - Step 1: P_full ships if it passes NW-P against Z (E2, margins unchanged).
    - Step 2: F1 on P_full (one V100, `--time 02:00:00`, Z re-scored in the same job): theta(a) = (1 - a) Z + a P_full of the two cross-encoders for a in {0.85, 0.7, 0.5}, the adapter of theta(a) the exact rank-64 LoRA by concatenation of Z's and P_full's adapters; the largest a that passes NW-P ships; VAL of each a is information. Disclosure: a below 1 mixes the weights with Z at 1 - a, which weakens every lever's effect, the KD term's included, not necessarily in proportion to a.
    - Step 3, only with hours left: a rerun of P_full with lowered lever shares, after its own short entry, on one V100 with Z re-scored on the same GPU after training (FINAL pub's rerun mode exempts this one-GPU run from the PROVISIONAL stop of `cells_s12_part3.py` lines 790 and 791), `--time 08:00:00`, then NW-P and F1 on it as in steps 1 and 2; the first that passes ships. Rerun R1: every V-MIX share outside v2 halved (honest groups v2 0.70, v3s-A 0.0625, v3s-B 0.0625, noex 0.10, name + first sentence 0.025, name 0.05; closed-inventory groups 80% inference text and 20% that mix); everything else as P_full. Rerun R2, only if R1 fails and hours remain: R1 plus wrapper share 0.15 in every eligible language (from 0.30 in en and de and from 0.20 in lt and pl) and typo share 0.05, stripping staying at 0.30: clean shares 0.7 x 0.85 x 0.95 = 0.565 for lt and pl, 0.85 x 0.95 = 0.8075 for en and 0.85 for de; the adapter's wrapper and typo shares of (4g) are lowered with them, since (4g) ties them to the cross-encoder's. If a teacher is used, R1 and R2 reuse the staged KD file, EX and plans of (6) unchanged, since their changes lie outside the fingerprint. Hours left means 100 minus the GPU-hours that sacct reports (allocated GPUs times elapsed time) for every job of the project's cluster account from job 245230 on ((12)); a rerun starts only if the hours left are at least 19.2 (the hours guard of (12) at ceilings: the rerun 8.0, its F1 2.0, F1 on P0 2.0, the low-a mixes of steps 6 and 7 2.0 and 2.0, P's test evaluation 1.4, the noise re-measure 1.8).
    - Step 4: P0 ships if it passes NW-P (computed in the P job).
    - Step 5: F1 on P0, as step 2.
    - Step 6, low-a mixes on P_full (the answer to item 8, (13)): F1 as in step 2 on P_full, the model of step 1, at a in {0.3, 0.15, 0.05} (one V100, `--time 02:00:00`, Z re-scored in the same job; about 0.5 GPU-hours per mix); the largest a that passes NW-P ships.
    - Step 7, low-a mixes on P0: the same on P0 at a in {0.3, 0.15, 0.05}; the largest a that passes NW-P ships. Disclosure for steps 6 and 7: at a 0.3 or below Z holds at least 0.7 of the weights, so the shipped model is close to Z and keeps only a small part of each lever's effect (step 6) or of what P0's public data add (step 7), not necessarily in proportion to a; the model card states a and this dilution ((10f)).
    - Step 8 (F4), only if no mix of steps 6 and 7 passes NW-P: Z is released under CC BY-NC 4.0 through its own packaging path, no public-model number exists, and, if anything is published, the failure is published with the records (E2's F4 text unchanged). This means that there is no public model v1, only the honest model. This outcome is practically impossible, because theta(a) approaches Z as a goes to 0 and NW-P compares with Z within margins of 0.010 to 0.020, but it is not a guarantee, so the step stays registered: a finished public model ships unless even the 0.05 mixes fail ((1)).
    - Order and omissions (delegated decision 8). WiSE (step 2) comes before a rerun because it needs no training (about 2 GPU-hours for three mixes against about 8 to 10 for a rerun with its F1) and was E2's registered first repair; it keeps every lever's data in the model, though weakened. The reruns keep every lever and lower only the shares named in decision 8, V-MIX first. E2's F2 (noise at its v1 value) and F3 (lighter caps with noise at its v1 value) are not run for P v1: both set the noise at its v1 value, which would remove the wrapper and typo levers and the adapter's noisy messages and halve the cross-encoder stripping share, against the requests of 18:45 and 18:47; F3's lighter caps were never registered without that noise change. The clause of the entry of 2026-10-06 17:18 that makes the noise-at-v1 run the first rerun is replaced for P v1 ((13)). Of two proposals of the checks of this entry, the mixes at low a before F4 are adopted with a 0.05 added (steps 6 and 7, the answer to item 8); the order F1 at a 0.85 and 0.7, then R1, then a 0.5, then F3's lighter caps with every lever kept, then P0, is not adopted (delegated decision 8, for the reasons above). Steps 6 and 7 follow P0 and its mixes, in the approved order: a mix that keeps at least half of P0 ships before one that keeps at most 0.3 of P_full. The noise reports of (10) never make a model that fails NW-P ship and never stop one that passes. V-V3S is then evaluated on the shipped model by E2's rule ((10c)). The shipped P is frozen (package sha256) before its single test evaluation.
  - (10) Reports and the steps after the choice (information only; none changes which model ships).
    - (10a) In the P job, for P_full, P0 and Z (and in their own jobs for a WiSE mix or rerun): every NW-P quantity and pool, VAL, GUARD, and the lever targets: e-mail (the EN_DEC and LTPL decision rows with `r2intent_noise.email_wrap`, pooled 0.5 / 0.5); one typo (the probe's typo1 change on those rows, seeds 0, 1 and 2, mean over seeds per row, pooled 0.5 / 0.5); names only (those rows scored with label names only, pooled 0.5 / 0.5); no diacritics (the LTPL rows with `r2intent_noise.strip_all`, row-pooled); V-37 (its fresh pools, English, pooled); V-IND (T_IND = 0.5 EN_IND + 0.5 LTPL_IND on its fresh pools and their lt and pl copies; the LTPL rows with nodia; nodia on the fresh copies; abcd_dev and the bitext_dev telco and retail ecommerce rows; ind_dev_seen_industry, which favours P_full by construction); the clean target 0.5 EN_DEC + 0.5 LTPL. Differences P_full minus P0 (what the levers add together) and P_full minus Z, with paired bootstrap intervals (2,000 resamples, seed 0, clusters (base pool, gold label); VAL: gold label). In the model card, for the shipped model against Z, each noisy target is reported as 'better on noisy text' (difference above 0) or not, and the clean EN_DEC and LTPL as 'within 0.010 of Z on clean text' (at least -0.010) or not, never as 'not worse', with the numbers and intervals, whatever they are.
    - (10b) Noise re-measure (NOISE-PUB, the measurement promised at 02:25), after the shipped model is chosen and before its test evaluation: models Z and the shipped P always, P0 and P_full each when it is not the shipped model; nothing under F4. Mode noise_probe_pub of `hpc/make_noise_notebook.py`; `r2intent_noise.py` unchanged (sha256 ae087879..., asserted): its change rules, draws, keys and seeds (typo1, typo2 and nodia_half seeds 0, 1 and 2, every other change seed 0), the e-mail templates (sha256 75ae5475603c13fcb1da3e09f1c64a60bce0457052f9289bc90ec7372f301075), the names-only variant and the statistics helpers. Rows: exactly those of the logged v1.1 probe (record `noise_probe.json` sha256 39cd653242c3093ab7b8826f6351c18e9ca00fbf7f4e51ae65d18a75a85fdc92, rows file `noise_probe_rows.parquet` sha256 9854711f980835611ec99b12c3bf37cbe67d873c31c9e520ece8bcf6094815eb), texts and label sets from data v2 and ML v2 with v2 label texts; decision rows are the v1.1 decision rows that data pub's DEV does not flag near_test (by base_row_id; the realised removal is recorded per pool), and a probe row in lt, pl, de or sk that a P training row matches exactly or at bge-m3 cosine >= 0.95 is not a decision row (counted; no training row is removed). Every change of the v1.1 probe is scored; the binding changes for the report are nodia, typo1, email and names. Scoring at w 60, k 5, tau null, v2 label texts (V-V3S's texts if it was applied). Gates: Z's re-measure must agree with the record per row on at least 0.99 of the observations of every group and change and reproduce every group accuracy within 0.005, otherwise the whole job is INVALID (no number used, one rerun after its own note); each P model's clean scores on the 1,800 clinc_dev rows must agree in top-1 with its stored DEV frame on at least 0.99 of the rows and within 0.003 in decision-row accuracy, otherwise that model's re-measure is INVALID (one rerun after its own note). The probe's guard of the entry of 2026-10-06 17:18 applies unchanged (allowlist, every test-named file refused, self-test); a record with a non-empty tests_opened is INVALID; `noise_compare.py` reads only this mode's rows files and records. A re-measure is FINAL when its gates passed and every pool of EN_NONCLINC and LTPL_ALL was scored clean and under nodia, typo1 (3 seeds), email and names. Groups: EN_NONCLINC (REAL4 plus the 9 synthetic English pools; 3,498 decision rows in the record before the removal) and LTPL_ALL (the lt and pl copies of clinc_dev and of the 9 synthetic pools; 4,305 rows in the record; `noise_probe.md`); the copies of clinc_dev stay in LTPL_ALL (selection used English clinc_dev only; disclosed). N(nodia) = LTPL_ALL with every diacritic removed; N(typo1), N(email), N(names) = 0.5 EN_NONCLINC + 0.5 LTPL_ALL under the change. Statistics: paired differences on the same rows and copies, the probe's cluster bootstrap (2,000 resamples, seed 0, clusters (base pool, English source row)), each 0.5 / 0.5 quantity computed inside each resample. Reported for the shipped P against Z (and against P0 or P_full when re-measured): under each change, on clean rows, and the difference of losses; the promise labels per change: better on noisy text (difference above 0 on N(c)), within 0.010 of Z on clean text (at least -0.010 on clean EN_NONCLINC and LTPL_ALL), no changed input clearly worse (at least -0.020 on each of the seven noisy parts); every group of at least 1,000 decision rows in which the shipped P is below Z by more than 0.020 under a change is named in the result entry and the model card. Caveat stated with these numbers: clinc_dev, bitext_dev, dstc11_insurance and sgd_unseen are in domain_seen_by_P, HINT3 is in P's data and clinc_dev is P's selection pool, so the comparison with Z on these rows is not held out, and the difference of losses is the readable robustness quantity. Resolution: the run-to-run standard error of a difference on these rows was estimated only in a working analysis on clean rows ((15)), and noisy parts have larger, unmeasured standard errors, so a small difference under a change is not evidence either way. Every job of this mode sets PREVIOUS_NOISE_RUN_JUSTIFICATION to this entry, which is the note that the one-run clause of the entry of 2026-10-06 17:18 requires; a second re-measure of one model needs its own note. Jobs: `--time 02:00:00`, CORE_DEADLINE_H 1.0, SESSION_DEADLINE_H 1.5, RESERVE_H 0.15; Z with the shipped P on two V100s, any further model on one; the mode, `noise_compare.py` and their sha256 values are logged before the first run.
    - (10c) V-V3S (no training): E2's rule unchanged, with the shipped model in M0's place: label-text mode v3s-A at inference, eligible only by the D18 rule, applied only if it passes VR against the shipped model; scored on DEV only.
    - (10d) K2 on a Kaggle T4 (method 3b, as entry T2 of 2026-10-07 14:56) for the shipped P, before any latency claim: single-message p50 and p90 at k 5, the rank-64 adapter if a WiSE mix ships, per-language latency (B-9); the package is uploaded privately; the Kaggle quota left after K0 and K1 covers it. If p50 is above 40 ms, no latency claim is made and this is disclosed; the shipped model does not change.
    - (10e) P's single test evaluation (E3): its own notebook, its sha256 logged in its own entry before it runs, `--gres=gpu:1`, the registered runtime without library pre-processing.
    - (10f) Packaging (on a pass): one file under CC BY-NC 4.0; before the package sha256 is frozen, the file names and metadata carry the model name R2Intent (decided in the working chat on 2026-10-05: the product is AnyIntent and its models are R2Intent; the answer to item 13 of (13); QUESTIONS_ru question 47), not the former internal name; NOTICE lists every source with training rows (E1 and this entry), the generators (Qwen2.5-14B-Instruct and Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct for the packs and V-37, EuroLLM-9B-Instruct-2512 for translations and wrappers, OPUS-MT), the teacher with revision and licence if the KD term was used, the HINT3 alterations file and the translation attributions; the model card states NW-P and its resolution, the ladder step that shipped, every report of (10), the single seed against the 3 seeds of PROTOCOL Metrics, the checkpoint selection on clinc_dev (a pool in domain_seen_by_P), the floor of (5), for a shipped WiSE mix its a and that it weakens every lever (at a 0.3 or below Z holds at least 0.7 of the weights, so little of each lever's effect remains), and the contamination statement of E3 as amended; zero-shot inference only (no few-shot block); Z is packaged next to P (E3).
    - (10g) Release items (not gates): the sacct export of every job is kept (QUESTIONS_ru question 24); the written confirmation from the cluster's operator, when available, is kept with the project's records (E2); the Kaggle datasets that hold weights stay private and are deleted after K2; cluster folders are cleaned at the end only with a yes given directly in the working chat on a listed set (QUESTIONS_ru question 53); the library README section on writing labels and the margin field (a confidence margin per prediction; AMENDMENTS_12 L-1) go with the library release entry.
    - (10h) Optional single-lever DEV runs (information only; the wishes of 2026-10-06 about 15:30, `docs/work/QUESTIONS_ru.md` line 150, and 16:26, and the offer of 19:07). After the shipped model is frozen, and only while the hours guard of (12) holds with P's test evaluation and the noise re-measure still protected, P0 plus one lever is trained on one V100 (FINAL pub's rerun mode with its one-GPU exemption, `--time 06:00:00`, the e-mail run `--time 07:00:00`; planning 4.5 to 5.5 V100-hours each, the e-mail run up to about 6.0), seed 0, with P0's recipe, data and checkpoint rule plus that lever's rows or changes exactly as in P_full (the wrappers and V-TYPO in the cross-encoder and the adapter) and no KD term, and is compared on DEV with P0 and with Z (their stored DEV frames, matched by base_row_id) on the NW-P quantities and that lever's target of (10a), with the intervals of (10a). Order: V-37, V-MIX, V-TYPO (the three named in the working chat), then the e-mail wrappers, then V-IND with the D pack; each run after its own short note. These runs are never shipped, never evaluated on a registered test set, never a teacher, miner, noise model or generator, and change no rule and no shipped model; they come before P seeds ((11)).
  - (11) Not in P_full, each with its reason.
    - The HN12 recipe and V-HNLT: HN12 lost clinc_dev against B, 0.8106 against 0.8176 (entry 2026-10-07 01:55), outside B's seed range 0.8164 to 0.8193 (entry 11:12), and lt 0.7326 against 0.7447 and clinc_dev@lt 0.7033 against 0.7294; its English gain was +0.0082 on EN_DEC at its own DEV-chosen w 30, already below VR's +0.010. The only number at P's w 60 is the unkept scratch recomputation of (15), seen before this exclusion was decided; it showed an English gain at w 60 below VR's +0.010 and informed the decision (delegated decision 2); its numbers are not cited. With it go V-SOUP and V-ROUTE (both need V-HNLT; V-ROUTE also breaks the one-file product and needs a detector and a latency check). The draft notes for V-HNLT and V-ROUTE are not logged.

Stronger lt and pl come instead from data already in P0 and P_full: MASSIVE pl-PL human rows (8 per intent and locale per epoch), EuroLLM lt rows of MASSIVE en-US (16 per intent per epoch; MASSIVE 1.1 has no Lithuanian locale), the EuroLLM lt and pl rows of every new source and of the packs, ML's lt and pl rows, and the stripping of (5) with V-ADIA.

The library items (optional e-mail cleaning, normalize_caps, long-text mode, abstain, calibration, the B-11 label-name cleanup, and CPU and ONNX latency reported as information) belong to the library release and its own entry; P's numbers are computed without them (E3 (b)). A check on human-written Lithuanian e-mails (QUESTIONS_ru question 36: about 300 to 500 anonymised e-mails, only with the clients' consent and a decision taken directly in the working chat) is an information item after release.

E1 keeps them out of P because they are P's DEV checks: DSTC11 banking and finance are NW-P's GUARD, dstc11_insurance is a pub-domain pool, and abcd_dev is an information pool of (10a); training on them would remove those checks.

Known before this entry and used in no rule: published Banking77 and MASSIVE results of Qwen3-Reranker-8B (RESEARCH_080 lever 9).
    - Result-informed choices: the composition of P_full, the exclusions of (11), the B12 rule, the floor and the checkpoint rule were fixed after the v1.1, stage-1.2, seeds, noise-probe and model D DEV results were known, and after the stage-1 test aggregates of entries 2026-10-05 14:10 and 17:45 were known; none uses a test row.
    - The teacher is chosen on the same DEV rows on which NW-P later judges P_full; the 0.015 margin limits the optimism of choosing the better of two. Several probe pools come from public data (the CLINC150 copies in LTPL, HINT3, SGD, Bitext) and most PIPE pools are Qwen-made synthetic texts; either can raise a candidate's S, and the margin is not adjusted for it. Gemma is probed on lists of 5 and scores training groups of 8. REAL has 311 decision rows, so S moves by about 0.0008 per REAL row. Not adopted from the teacher research: a DEV-fitted temperature, a position-prior correction, gold NLL as the selection metric and a KD pilot against label smoothing.
    - Model D, whose data overlaps P_full's (data 1.2, the Bitext dom verticals, NLU++ banking, MultiDoGO and the D pack), was below Z on matched DEV rows by 0.0131 on LTPL and 0.0152 on clinc_dev, and by 0.0086 on EN_DEC without domain_seen_by_D (entry 2026-10-07 17:56). P_full may need the repair ladder.
    - The floor of (5) lowers the lt and pl e-mail wrapper share of P_full from 0.30 to 0.20, the price of keeping half of the lt and pl groups clean, although the probe's e-mail loss was larger in lt and pl than in English ((4d)); stripping stays at the registered 0.30 in P0 and P_full, and P_full's adapter uses the same shares ((4g)). This was chosen at 21:09 over the common factor of delegated decision 5, which had lowered P_full's lt and pl stripping share to 0.2598 ((13)). The wrapper and typo parts of the adapter were written into this entry on the answer to item 10, after the checks of its drafts, and were not checked as a draft of their own; the CPU smoke of patch P tests them ((14)).
    - MASSIVE 1.1 has no Lithuanian locale; P's Lithuanian training text is machine-translated or EuroLLM-generated (no human-written Lithuanian intent data with a usable licence exists, PLAN_80 2.1), and every Lithuanian and Polish DEV number is on machine-translated text.
    - The 12 V-IND seeds, the V-E and V-TEACH prompts, the industry exclusions and the probe objects were written for this project with knowledge of the registered label inventories, the stage-1 test aggregates and the model D DEV result; the 9 D-pack seeds were written for this project before model D existed (entry 2026-10-07 14:39). They are chosen to avoid the registered label sets, not to match them. Qwen2.5 models may have seen public intent data in pretraining; the 0.90 filter removes near copies only, and the money-side intents of V-IND and the D pack sit close in meaning to banking labels.
    - EuroLLM-9B-Instruct-2512 was trained and is stored in bf16, which the V100 lacks, so the wrappers are generated in fp16 as ML 1.2's EuroLLM rows were; its card says it is not aligned to human preferences, so every wrapper passes the rule filters, and the lt and pl wrappers may be read by the person who gave the delegation. The wrapper target uses one fixed probe wrapper per language written for this project, and filter (e)(i) and the prompt were written knowing its words; real e-mails with quoted threads, disclaimers or long bodies are not covered.
