English checkpoint for English locales, multilingual for every other locale.
  - Format (the release's benchmark scripts at 3cf26cbc: research/benchmarks/tournament/benchmark.py sha256 51f7217e5603668e214c7cdda54e15ef870aa65a399efda6e084dafb48de046b and research/scripts/bench_apps.py sha256 dc78587045ee077b50ff7f99b05ce49a9837ed4c0721ee78e6e203fac707d791): one choice question with id q, criteria {name: None} (names only, no descriptions), the state a JSON object with one text field. Banking77: field message, "Which banking intent does `message` express?" (both scripts). CLINC150 banking and credit_cards, HWU64, MASSIVE, xSID, MTOP and MInDS-14: field message, "Which intent does `message` express?" (benchmark.py's CLINC150 and MASSIVE wording; the release has no format for HWU64, xSID, MTOP or MInDS-14). SIB-200: field article, "What is the topic of `article`?" (bench_apps.py's topic question). Options: the readable label names of the frozen description files (the look's m3_name texts) in the look's label order (sorted raw keys); for Banking77 this is bench_apps.py's sorted() order; it is not benchmark.py's label-id order for Banking77, CLINC150 and MASSIVE, and the run never claims that format for the order. The same order in every setting.
  - Calls: one message per call (benchmark.py's call): (1) agent.predict(state, q) with nothing else set; (2) agent.predict(state, q, head_max_len=512); (3) laya.predict_tournament(agent, state, q) with group_size 16 (the default) and no other argument. On a V100 Laya's own rule autocasts in fp16 with fp32 parameters (asserted at load). A row whose call raises ValueError is counted wrong (pred -1, error recorded; benchmark.py's rule); any other exception fails the cell. A row with any non-finite probability is counted in the cell's non-finite count; a cell with a non-finite count above 0 or any CPU fallback is flagged, reported and never compared (the look's rule), and nothing is recomputed. Failed cells are never retried.
  - EmbeddingGemma 2: google/embeddinggemma-2 at 914f7f89142e33e77833254d9c9b90c3cef7303b (the only commit; not gated; Apache-2.0 in the card's YAML header; model.safetensors sha256 197a32965d4b1105faf060417baa899e193fb73cd401f42ec9295234d5553d79), text path through sentence-transformers with no vision or audio tower, fp32 with no autocast, max_seq_length 512 (the look's embedders), prompts from the card: message with SearchQuery "task: search result | query: ", label text with Document "title: none | text: "; rows eg2_name (label names) and eg2_desc ("name: description" of the frozen files), top-1 cosine of normalised 768-d vectors; non-finite rows counted and set to 0 as in the look.
  - Cells, in this order (106 in all): (a) Laya (1) on the 50 registered (set, locale) cells in the look's set and locale order; EmbeddingGemma 2 on the same 50 cells (T3b's 'after Laya's cell (a)' read as right after it); (b) Laya (2) on Banking77, HWU64, CLINC150 banking and credit_cards (English checkpoint) and xSID lt (multilingual), in this listed order; (c) Laya (3) on Banking77 and HWU64.
  - GPUs and budget: one V100 (--gres=gpu:1, --time 05:00:00), so the job holds at most 5.0 V100-hours. A cell starts only before 4.75 h from the job start; a cell still running at 4.83 h is stopped and listed 'not completed (budget)' with its rows dropped; later cells are 'not run (budget)'. Expected 0.6 to 1.2 V100-hours (an estimate). A separate model cache (hf_cache_t3b) and a separate environment (.venv_t3b); the look's environment and cache are unchanged.
  - One run: the run is used up once the test store is opened; an atomic lock out/.lock_t3b is created then and kept. A job that stops before the store is opened (for example a model load failure) may be resubmitted once after a short note. The look folder is the single folder among out/look_* and results/step2_look* whose look_results_manifest.json sha256 equals R2INTENT_LOOK_DONE (exactly one); every look file is read only after its sha256 equals the manifest's, never written, and re-hashed at the end. Rows, ids and label spaces come from the look's own code, extracted byte for byte from the pinned look notebook (1a494298...), and are checked against the look's headline rows element by element before the first cell.
  - Reported as T3b, with the look's functions: accuracy and macro-F1 with row intervals and label-cluster intervals for both (2,000 resamples, seed 0), the look's subsets (an empty subset is skipped as the look skips it), per-row predictions keyed by the look's row ids, headline minus each row on the same rows, and per (set, locale) whether Laya (1), eg2_name or eg2_desc is strictly above the look's recorded best registered baseline (if so, headline minus it is quoted; a flagged or not-run cell, or a cell with no recorded best baseline, gets 'no comparison'). Labels: 'Laya'; 'Laya, head_max_len=512 (not the release default)'; 'Laya, tournament mode (not the release default)'; 'EmbeddingGemma 2, label names'; 'EmbeddingGemma 2, name: description'; all under 'post-look baseline, registered before the look'.
  - Smoke runs before the real run, on generated stand-ins only (no test file and no look output opened): with stub models on the CPU, with the real checkpoints offline on the CPU partition, and a short GPU smoke on one V100 (outside the 5.0 budget, about 0.25 V100-hours).

It loads r2intent-data-v3 and r2intent-ml-v3 and refuses them unless their manifest sha256 values equal the stage-1.2 decision's inputs (91993559... and 9ece4703...), refuses any dom, dompack, pub or look folder and any added-source section, and refuses to start unless the recipe equals B's as JD trained model D (adopted B, recipe v1a, sampler v1, ONE0 with its adapter, 2 epochs, selection k 8, evaluations every 400 steps with v1's 12% rule, ML_STRIP_P 0.15, seed 0, AD_STEPS 3,000, w 60, k 5, tau null, v2 label texts, no label-mode mix, no L1 adapter, v2 negatives). Reading of E5 (14) 'Data notebook: PUB_BASE and the B12 variant of (3)': the variant sits where (3)(i) puts it, on the stage-1.2 FINAL dom path; the data notebook carries PUB_BASE. The dom-source assertions are recorded as not applicable (no source is added), no DEV pool is trained_in_D or domain_seen_by_D, and model D's label 'trades general accuracy for banking' is not applied. The record (final_decision_<run>.json with dom_data_variant base) keeps the FINAL dom arm name D (files stage12_cross_d, stage12_adapter_d.safetensors, stage12_dev_scores_D_<run>.parquet) and adds a section b12: ltpl_b12_minus_z, unrounded, equal to the honest comparison's D_minus_honest.ltpl on the matched decision rows (Z = configs.B, its stored DEV frame), the pools compared and not compared, the decision rows, fires (true if the value is below -0.010; a value equal to -0.010 does not fire) and fires_null_reason. A record whose fires is null (the record is not FINAL, the honest comparison failed, or no LTPL value exists on the matched rows) counts as a run that did not end FINAL for E5 (3): one identical rerun follows after its own note, and if that also fails the honest part is data 1.2 and ML 1.2 (disclosed). A run qualifies if it scores every stand-in prompt of both orders and its letter log-probabilities agree with the base run's (the reference checks' thresholds); the fastest qualifying run sets the pins (flags, slots, context) for A2 and for the teacher scoring job. No pins and no speed are recorded unless every stand-in prompt has 330 to 420 tokens; a stand-in list of 8 (letters A to H, both orders) must fit the smallest context capacity of the grid. - 2026-10-11 22:01, rule B: an amendment of E5 (9) (decided in the working chat at 21:59), logged before any NW-P result of the P job is read. It does not change the test sets, metrics or success criteria. It does not change the P job, its notebook (whose sha256 is logged for the P job), the WiSE and rerun jobs, or any file under `tools/step1_stage12_build/src`.
  - The clause. P_full, and any model built from P_full (its WiSE mixes of step 2, the reruns R1 and R2 of step 3 and their mixes, the low-a mixes of step 6), ships only if it ALSO passes NW-P against P0 of the P job. The comparison uses the same quantities, rows, groups, w, k and margins, and the same function `r2intent_pub_lib.nwp` (lines 2748 to 2775), with P0 in Z's place. This extra condition applies only while P0 itself passes NW-P against Z. If P0 fails NW-P against Z, a model built from P_full is compared with Z only, as registered. Steps 4, 5 and 7 (P0 and its mixes) and step 8 are unchanged.
  - Where it applies. Steps 1, 2, 3 and 6 of E5 (9), for these models: P_full of the P job, or of its worker rerun rerun_pfull under E5 (8); the mixes of wise_pfull; R1 and R2 (rerun_r1, rerun_r2) and the mixes of wise_r1 and wise_r2; the mixes of wise_low_pfull. P0 is the P job's P0, or the P0 of the worker rerun rerun_p0 when the P job's worker of Z and P0 did not end FINAL (E5 (8)); "P0 passes NW-P against Z" is the NW-P result of P0's own job. Step 6 is reached only when P0 fails NW-P against Z at step 4, so rule B never binds there; it is named for completeness.
  - The comparison. NW-P of a model against P0 checks every binding clause: en_dec, ltpl, en_unseen and ltpl_unseen at 0.010; lt_unseen, pl_unseen and desk_unseen at 0.015; guard at 0.020. Each clause passes when the model is at least P0 minus the margin, compared unrounded. The clauses are computed on the decision rows of the union DEV in the records' NW-P groups, at the records' w 60 and k 5. A NaN, an empty group or different pool sets fail. Both models are read from their stored DEV frames (E5 (8)), and their DEV rows (pool, row id, base_row_id, gold label) must be equal. Only a model that ended FINAL in its record is compared, and for P_full only the run the ladder uses; a model that did not end FINAL is never shipped by the ladder (its frame may be partial after a stop), so it is listed as not compared.
  - Two readings of the WiSE steps, approved with this entry (no job code changes):
    - (a) At a WiSE step, the mix judged against P0 is the one the WiSE job chose and packaged: the largest a that passes NW-P against Z. If it fails against P0, the step gives no shipped model and the ladder goes on, even when a smaller a passes both comparisons, because that mix has no package. The ladder state names such a mix.
    - (b) When a model built from P_full passes NW-P against Z but fails against P0, the WiSE step on it (step 2 for P_full, the F1 of step 3 for R1 or R2) is not run, because the registered WiSE job refuses a source model that passed NW-P (`cells_s12_part2.py` lines 603 to 609). The ladder goes on to step 3 if hours are left, then to step 4. So while rule B applies, the ladder ships either a model built from P_full that passes against both Z and P0, or P0 at step 4.
  - The tool. `tools/step1_stage12_build/nwp_vs_p0.py` (sha256 d67c943b7a2065ca026b9078a3333daf3ad6e010c8891873b9955ac7f91d92d9, commit bcb7ffd; the local check `check_rule_b_local.py` 055cdf2c71e961fa0a69b9b1b6fdff88d95937dde60570e94c789567820a5b32 passes 37 of 37, check_pub_local 178 of 178) runs on the CPU, with no model, after each job of the ladder from the P job on. It rebuilds the union DEV pools and NW-P groups by the notebook's calls from the DEV files of the layer named by the records' union_dev.top, each checked against the records, and refuses unless the groups equal every record's. It recomputes NW-P against Z of every model from its stored DEV frame and refuses unless the result equals the record's stored result exactly, in every clause, pool and number. It computes NW-P against P0 for every model built from P_full that the ladder can ship. It writes the record `nwp_vs_p0_<run id>_<time>.json` (kind nwp_vs_p0, status FINAL, with its inputs and their sha256, the rule text, p_full_run and rule_b_applies) and prints its sha256. It refuses a smoke record mixed with real ones, a frame whose sha256 differs from its record, models of different DEV sets, and a worker rerun given without need or needed and missing. Every input goes through the stage 1.2 test-file guard, and no test file is opened.
  - The ladder and the release. `tools/step1_stage12_build/ladder_state.py` (sha256 c0424473875e2697480673e4e44a1d24bfa14a60a1b2f48ff4bc4e48c08a0946) is run with `--rule-b <record>:<sha256>` for every state from step 1 on; before step 1 (a worker rerun of E5 (8) is due) the tool refuses and the ladder is run without the flag, because rule B plays no part there. The ladder refuses unless the rule B record names exactly the records it was given, its P0 and its run of P_full. If rule B applies and a model built from P_full has no result against P0, the state is "run nwp_vs_p0.py", never a ship. The state names the condition that failed (NW-P against Z or NW-P against P0) and the failing clauses. Without `--rule-b` its output is unchanged, byte for byte. The ladder state pinned for the PACK build is the one made with the last rule B record logged; the PACK spec pins that record under the key rule_b, and `tools/pack_build/r2_pack.py` (sha256 5de3896115dea3120223663615a6d4bea293baef6b730f73ece76b9b4038b75e) then refuses a ladder state not made with it, a record that does not name the shipped record, and a shipped model built from P_full that fails against P0 while rule B applies. The freeze is run with the same `--rule-b`, so the frozen manifest names the rule B record. Each result entry of the ladder logs the rule B record's sha256.
  - Hours. The tool uses no GPU. Rule B never adds a WiSE job on a model that passes NW-P against Z (reading (b)). It can add the reruns of step 3: when P_full passes NW-P against Z but fails against P0, the registered ladder ships P_full at step 1, while under rule B the ladder goes to step 3. There R1 (ceiling 8.0 V100-hours) may run, then its F1 (wise_r1, 2.0) only when R1 fails NW-P against Z, then R2 and its F1 the same way if R1 does not ship: at most 20.0 V100-hours in all. Each starts only under the hours guard of E5 (12) (at least 19.2 hours left). At the planning highs of E5 (12) about 16.9 hours are left when step 3 is reached, so a rerun starts only if the earlier jobs use at least 2.3 GPU-hours less than their planning highs; otherwise the ladder goes to step 4 and ships P0.
  - Expected effect on the risk of a false fail. A second comparison adds its own chance of failing by noise. The entry of 2026-10-09 12:06 estimated that a model equal to Z fails NW-P by chance with a probability of roughly 0.1 to 0.2 (E2's method on Z's three seeds). Assume the same figure for P_full against an equally good P0. Then a P_full equal in quality to both models fails step 1 by noise alone with a probability of roughly 0.1 to 0.2 under the registered rule, and of at most roughly 0.2 to 0.35 under rule B. The two failures are positively correlated, because both comparisons share P_full's scores, so the true figure lies between these ranges. The figure for P_full against P0 is not measured. Under rule B, such a false fail costs P_full: with P0 passing NW-P against Z, the ladder ships P0 at step 4, unless a rerun of step 3 (only with hours left, at the cost above) passes both comparisons. What rule B buys: no model built from P_full ships when it is below P0 by more than the NW-P margin on any binding clause, that is, when the levers together lose against the public data alone.
  - Reporting. The result entry of the ladder states that rule B was in force and whether it applied. It also states the NW-P against P0 of the shipped model, or why P0 shipped. The model card lists the rule B record among its records; whether its text names rule B is decided with the PACK texts.

The yes to the DEV job of (4): 2026-10-12 22:12. The not-read remainder of `scores` in (1) was decided under the delegation of 2026-10-06 15:30, after the review of the N1 code found that 5 values summing to 1 cannot be calibrated on messages whose right intent was not read (8); its form was widened and its clause S6 added, under the same delegation, after a review of that design found that the simulation had tested an ideal remainder and not the fitted one (8); it was night, so this was not reported in the working chat before this entry; it is reported there in the morning and may still be changed before any calibration number exists (`newfx_calibrate.py`, which computes the first calibration number, runs only after that report has been read by the person who gave the delegation). This entry brings forward the library item "calibration on the DEV of the shipped model" (library entry of 2026-10-06 20:04; E5 (11) 'The library items (... calibration ...) belong to the library release and its own entry'); P's registered numbers are still computed without it (E3 (b)).
  - (1) Definitions. `AnyIntent.check(text, intent, threshold=0.5)` scores the single pair directly, without a label list, without touching the current label set or the shared label cache (its intent vectors sit in a private in-memory cache): cosine of the message and the intent's label text (embedding pass, adapter on), cross logit of the pair (adapter off, `cross_texts`), fused score s = cross + w x cosine at the frozen w 60, probability = 1 / (1 + exp(-(a s + b))), yes = probability >= threshold; `intent` is a name, a {"name", "description"} dict or a (name, description) tuple, and a list is refused; meaning: the chance that the message expresses the intent when a match and a non-match are taken as equally likely before reading it (only 0.5 is measured); the README and the card give the share of right "yes" answers at a match rate of 1/2, 1/5 and 1/20 from the held-out true-yes and true-no rates (the prior table). `AnyIntent.scores(text)` returns a `ScoresResult`: `values` (for every intent of the current label set, in set_labels order), `remainder`, `read` (the names of the read intents, retrieval order) and `label`. With f_1 to f_5 the fused scores of the 5 intents classify reads with the message (the frozen k), f_(1) the highest and f_(5) the lowest of them and L the number of intents in the label set, on a label set of 6 intents or more each read intent j gets the logit z_j = (f_j - f_(5)) / T and a virtual sixth candidate the logit z_v = gamma + eta x (f_(1) - f_(5)) + kappa x log(L - 5); a read intent's value is exp(z_j) / (sum over the 5 of exp(z_i) + exp(z_v)) and the remainder is exp(z_v) over the same sum; every intent not read gets 0.0, which means "not read"; on a label set of 5 intents or fewer every intent is read, the values are the softmax of f_j / T over all of them and the remainder is 0 by definition; the values sum to 1 minus the remainder. It runs classify's own passes (`classify(..., cascade=False, return_scores=True)`), so within one call `label`, the intent with the largest value, is classify's label. Meaning: a read intent's value is the chance that this intent is the right one, and the remainder the chance that the right intent is one of the given intents the model did not read, both on every message and assuming one of the given intents is right (the remainder is not a "none of these" answer). The calibration file is read at the first call of either function (a Hugging Face model fetches only that file at its commit), never at load; a malformed file raises only in the two functions. Both refuse, at call time: no calibration file or no block for the function; a w different from the file's (a load override); ONNX int8; a calibration file whose model_sha256 is not the loaded model.safetensors'. `check` also refuses a label text mode different from the file's; `scores` also refuses an active w or k or a label set's text mode different from the file's, few-shot mode, duplicate names and no labels. Both warn when an intent has no description (the rule covers intents written with a description). Not a "none of these" mode.

Every assertion holds; 33 normalised texts are shared by two base pools (dstc11_banking_guard with dstc11_finance_guard, 31; ind_dev_unseen_telecom_operator with synth_dev_unseen_yoga_studio, 2; 66 rows), each on one side only.
  - (4) The DEV job (mode newfx_dev, one V100, `--time 01:30:00`; its notebook sha256, smoke, staging and sbatch line in a short entry before it runs, and the OK of 2026-10-12 22:12 from the working chat). On the shipped package r2intent_p_full (model.safetensors ba8d2e74...): a gate re-scoring the 1,800 clinc_dev rows and their lt and pl copies with the P job's scorer and libraries byte for byte against the stored frame (candidates equal except cosine near ties of 2e-3, cross logits within max(0.05, 1% of the largest), top-1 agreement at least 0.99 and decision-row accuracy within 0.003; else a technical stop, one rerun after its note, D22 for a bug); the split of (3); for every kept row the gold pair, the same-side uniform negatives, the non-gold top 5 and the name-only gold and uniform pairs scored with the same scorer into `newfx_dev_pairs_<run>.parquet`; the classify identity of (2); the new library's raw scores on every row of clinc_dev, clinc_dev@lt and clinc_dev@pl with the engines auto (fast) and eager (classify's 5 candidates with return_scores, and the raw pair scores of the gold and uniform pairs) into `newfx_dev_libscores_<run>.parquet`. No calibration number is computed in the job. tests_opened must be empty.
  - (5) Models and fitting (CPU tool `tools/newfx_build/newfx_calibrate.py`, run once). `scores`: T, gamma, eta and kappa jointly by maximum likelihood over all calibration rows, each row once, the target being the gold's position among the 5 read intents or "not read" (retrieval rank 5 or more); with beta = 1/T the logits are linear in (beta, gamma, eta, kappa) and the negative log-likelihood convex: Newton iterations in float64 from (1 / sd of the positive gaps f_j - f_(5), 0, 0, 0) with step halving, every step below 1e-10 within 100 iterations; the fit fails S0 (a result) when fewer than 20 calibration rows of sets over 5 intents have their gold not read or fewer than 20 have it read (the record states both counts), when the information matrix scaled to unit diagonal is not positive definite (a parameter not identified), without convergence, when T is not positive, when T is not strictly inside 10^-1.3 to 10^1.3, or when a parameter is not finite; the record states the standard errors as information; a non-finite read score or a target out of range is a technical stop. `check`: (a, b) by weighted maximum likelihood (Newton, float64, no regularisation, steps below 1e-10 within 100 iterations); a fit that does not converge, or a <= 0, fails C0 (a result, not a technical failure). One model per function; no other model, feature set, population, split, per-language parameter or remainder (revision 3's two-parameter remainder included) is tried on any part of DEV. The tool refuses to run when any newfx_calibration_*.json exists under out/ or results/ unless a D22 note heading is given; after any failed clause a D22 rerun needs a defect shown on stand-ins, and both records are reported; it refuses test-named inputs and recomputes the job's split.
  - (6) Metrics on the held-out part. ECE with 15 equal-width bins ([j/15, (j+1)/15), 1.0 in the last bin): top-label ECE of `scores` (the top value against "the top intent is the gold"), candidate ECE of `scores` (every read value against "this candidate is the gold", each row's values weighted 1/(number read), rows whose gold was not read included; the remainder is not one of these values), the remainder ECE of `scores` (S6: the remainder against "the gold was not read" on rows of label sets over 5 intents, each row once, pooled and per label-set size band 6 to 20, 21 to 40, 41 or more) and recall@5 (information: the share of held-out rows whose gold was read), ECE of `check` (the pair probability against "the intent is the gold", weights of (3)). Balanced accuracy of `check` at 0.5 (the mean of the true-yes and true-no rates). Consistency on the clinc_dev rows and copies, each engine: the share of rows (every value and the remainder) or pairs whose library probability is within 0.01 of the job scorer's with the fitted parameters, and the difference of the ECE computed from both. Intervals: cluster bootstrap, clusters (base pool, gold label) of the held-out rows, 2,000 resamples, numpy default_rng(0), each metric recomputed per resample on the fixed bins, 95% interval by the 2.5th and 97.5th percentiles; for `check`, two-way weights (a negative pair weighted by its message cluster's draw count times its negative intent's cluster draw count; a positive pair by its message cluster's count).
  - (7) Ship rule, fixed here (decided in the working chat: the thresholds of the design). `scores` ships only if: S0 the joint fit of (5) succeeded (at least 20 calibration rows of sets over 5 intents read and 20 not read, identified and converged, T strictly inside 10^-1.3 to 10^1.3, every parameter finite), on every held-out row the top value's intent equals the fused top-1 at w 60, k 5 that the tool computes from the frame's scores (a check of the code that picks the top value), the values and the remainder in [0, 1] with 0.0 beyond the read intents and summing to 1 within 1e-9, and the remainder exactly 0 on every row whose label set has 5 intents or fewer; S1 pooled top-label ECE <= 0.030 and its 97.5th percentile <= 0.050; S2 top-label ECE <= 0.050 in EN and in LTPL; S3 top-label ECE <= 0.050 in EN_unseen and in LTPL_unseen; S4 for each engine at least 99% of the consistency rows have every value and the remainder within 0.01 of the scorer's, and the ECE from the library's scores within 0.010 of the scorer's; S5 pooled candidate ECE over the read values <= 0.030 and its 97.5th percentile <= 0.050; S6 pooled remainder ECE <= 0.030 and its 97.5th percentile <= 0.050, and the remainder ECE <= 0.050 in each label-set size band 6 to 20, 21 to 40 and 41 or more (point bounds; a band without held-out rows fails). recall@5 is information, not a clause. `check` ships only if: C0 the fit converged and a > 0; C1 pooled ECE <= 0.030 and its 97.5th two-way percentile <= 0.050; C2 ECE <= 0.050 in EN and in LTPL; C3 balanced accuracy at 0.5 >= 0.85 and its 2.5th two-way percentile >= 0.80; C4 ECE <= 0.050 in EN_unseen and in LTPL_unseen; C5 for each engine at least 99% of the gold and uniform consistency pairs have a library probability within 0.01 of the scorer's, and the ECE from the library's scores within 0.010 of the scorer's. Group bounds are point bounds. A flag of S0 or C0 is a Python or numpy bool and a metric value is a finite number (a string or a bool is not), else the clause fails. Reasons, fixed before any calibration number of P: an average probability error of a few points does not change a user's decisions at the usual cut-offs, 5 points can; one parameter set serves every language and P is weaker in lt and pl (LTPL 0.7529 against EN_DEC 0.8417, entry of 11:02), so each language group must be calibrated; users write new intents, so the intents P never saw must be calibrated; users run the library's fast fp16 engine, not the job's scorer, so the shipped library must reproduce the probabilities; `scores` gives a value per read intent, so every read value is checked, on every held-out row, the rows whose right intent was not read included (the remainder makes that possible); the remainder's meaning is promised to users too, and a remainder too high on some label-set sizes and too low on others cancels in the pooled clauses, so S6 checks it pooled and per size band; chance for `check` is 0.5 and P picks the right intent out of 4 to 63 at about 0.80 on DEV (known before this rule), so a yes or no for one intent against a uniformly drawn other intent must reach at least that reliability. Hard negatives are information. The rule runs once; a failed clause is a result, not a bug, and nothing is changed after it.
  - (8) What was known, and the noise of the rule. Known to the project when the thresholds were set, and read for the design's revision of 2026-10-12 before this entry: the v1.1 noise probe's calibration (PROTOCOL entry of 2026-10-06 17:50; `results/noise_probe_v11/noise_probe.md`; another model, one temperature 2.7 fitted in-sample on EN5, 10 bins): EN5 ECE 0.087 at T 1 and 0.016 at T 2.7, LTPL 0.0297, PL 0.0395, LTPL_ALL 0.0355, PL_ALL 0.0407; out of scope AUROC 0.80 to 0.84 (margin) and 0.82 to 0.91 (top cross logit); B-5 (h)'s evidence (v1.1, T 2.5): EN13 0.086 to 0.018, lt 0.134 to 0.037, pl 0.156 to 0.044. The first version of the design did not cite them; it cannot be ruled out that they were seen then; no threshold was changed after they were read. No calibration number of P exists: the noise re-measure records (jobs 245832 and 245823) and noise_compare hold none (checked by key names and headings only). A counts-only simulation (no score; the manifests' pool sizes with the exclusions of (3); calibrated stand-in predictors with and without cluster effects; 60 replicates; `newfx/sim/power_sim.py` of the design) gave held-out estimates of a calibrated predictor of 0.007 to 0.010 pooled and 0.009 to 0.024 per group for `scores` and 0.003 to 0.011 for `check`, and every clause S1 to S3 and C1 to C4 passed in every replicate. The same simulation on the realised DEV counts of (3), after the reviews of the N1 code and of the remainder design, with the remainder of (1) (`tools/newfx_build/newfx_power.py` sha256 524fe15218f5cd87c0777c5451072255f366e0b01167bcae2765a568fe848995 and `tools/newfx_build/src/r2intent_newfx.py` eda24fa3461a9c46c0dca757c859b980f59557f8e53c2ef8d25ae2f2d8277477 at commit e5d5f55, model-free checks `tools/newfx_build/check_newfx_local.py` 904bb32ae34c60fef6fbe9dd7690e4a08d6b6100ef9f5da265327d3bff34c1fb with 251 of 251 passing on stand-ins, on synthetic data and on the DEV files; the population, split and draws of (3); cluster effects sd 0 and 0.5, 60 replicates, 200 resamples, seed 0; record `newfx_power_realised_r4.json` sha256 77a4c22344b29edef54311879708f9b8c84510864a282ff7dded787cf5bf89d2), with two `scores` stand-ins. The ideal stand-in (the gate) is calibrated by construction with its remainder at recall@5 1.00, 0.95, 0.90, 0.87, 0.85 and 0.80 (on rows of label sets over 5 intents the gold is not read with mean probability 1 - recall@5; the remainder is the chance of that given a per-row variable the predictor sees and the design's fit cannot, and each value the chance of its intent); it shows only that the rule does not fail a calibrated function by chance. Pass probability, sd 0 and sd 0.5: S1, S2, S3, S5 and S6 1.000 and 1.000 at every one of these recall@5 levels; C1, C2, C3 and C4 1.000 and 1.000 at a true balanced accuracy of 0.90 and at 0.87 (independent of recall@5). Mean (95th percentile over replicates) of the held-out estimate over both settings and every recall@5 level: `scores` top-label ECE pooled 0.009 to 0.012 (up to 0.016), EN 0.013 to 0.018 (up to 0.024), LTPL 0.016 to 0.018 (up to 0.026), EN_unseen 0.018 to 0.024 (up to 0.035), LTPL_unseen 0.019 to 0.024 (up to 0.033), its pooled 97.5th percentile 0.018 to 0.022 (up to 0.025), candidate ECE 0.003 (up to 0.005) with its 97.5th percentile 0.006 to 0.007 (up to 0.008), remainder ECE pooled 0 at recall@5 1.00 and 0.003 to 0.009 below it (up to 0.013), its 97.5th percentile 0.008 to 0.018 (up to 0.022), largest size band 0.008 to 0.020 (up to 0.027); `check` ECE pooled 0.004 to 0.006 (up to 0.009), groups 0.006 to 0.011 (up to 0.016), pooled 97.5th two-way percentile 0.009 to 0.015 (up to 0.018), 2.5th two-way percentile of the balanced accuracy 0.892 to 0.895 (true 0.90) and 0.862 to 0.865 (true 0.87). This entry is logged only because every clause passes there with probability at least 0.95 in both settings at every recall@5 level. The design-fit stand-in (information, no gate; 60 replicates per setting) runs the design's own fit of (5) on the 7,081 calibration rows of synthetic fused scores (3 x the ideal stand-in's read logits, so the true T is 3 at sd 0) and the clauses on the 13,405 held-out rows, with the gold not read with probability sigmoid(a + c ell + sigma u + size log(L - 5) + B) (ell minus the log of the sum over the read intents of exp(g_j - g_(5)), g the read logits; u per row and B per intent cluster unseen) in five kinds: within the remainder's family (c 1), half tied to the gaps (c 0.5, sigma 1), independent of the gaps (c 0, sigma 1), over tied (c 2) and size dependent (c 0.5, sigma 1, size 1): the remainder of (1) passes S1, S2, S3 and S5 with probability 1.000 and S6 with at least 0.967 in every kind at recall@5 0.95, 0.90, 0.87, 0.85 and 0.80 (top-label ECE 0.010 to 0.016, remainder ECE 0.003 to 0.022, largest size band up to 0.038 at the 95th percentile, fitted T 2.99 to 3.00 at sd 0), while the two-parameter remainder of the design's revision 3 (an offset delta at the lowest read score, which these misses fit only in the first kind) passes S1 with probability as low as 0.000 (independent misses at recall@5 0.90: top-label ECE 0.043 to 0.045) and S6 as low as 0.000 (misses that depend on the size: band ECE 0.056 to 0.058 at recall@5 0.90 while S1 and S5 pass). Misses that depend on what the remainder does not use (the absolute score level, the language, the domain) are not covered by the stand-ins; S6 measures them on DEV. Why the remainder: in the first version of the design the 5 values of a row summed to 1 whatever T was, so a row whose gold was not among them added 1/5 of error to the candidate ECE, which then had the floor (1 - recall@5) x 0.9952 / 5 (0.9952: the share of held-out rows whose label set has more than 5 intents), 0.030 at a recall@5 of 0.849; in the run of the N1 code (commit 732b28e, record `newfx_power_realised_732b28e.json` sha256 04572b3ab06fadab7d28aa6240a7e9f05bc374c4479cfabe5d684c868a2c6449) S5 then passed with probability 0.600 and 0.650 at recall@5 0.85, whatever the calibration, and the meaning "the chance this intent is right" was false on those rows. The other fix considered, values summing to 1 with S5 measured only on rows whose gold was read and recall@5 stated once, was not chosen: its values would mean "the chance this intent is right if the right intent was read", a condition a user cannot see, and they would be too high exactly on the messages the model missed; in the same simulation, values exactly right under that condition have a top-label ECE of 0.038, 0.074 and 0.111 at recall@5 0.95, 0.90 and 0.85 (S1 fails) and a candidate ECE on all rows of 0.010, 0.020 and 0.030. Why its form changed after that (the review of the design's revision 3, before any number of P): with one offset delta, the remainder is a fixed multiple of the lowest read value, calibrated only if the chance of a miss follows the gaps to the lowest read score; the first realised simulation's stand-in could not show that, because its remainder knew what drives the misses. P's recall@5 on DEV was not read for this entry; on any set of rows it is at least P's top-1 accuracy there, since classify answers among the 5 read intents (on DEV EN_DEC 0.8417 and LTPL 0.7529, entry of 11:02). The thresholds of S1 to S5 and C1 to C5 are unchanged; S6 adds a condition.
  - (9) Information on DEV (no clause), in the record `newfx_calibration_<run>.json`: Brier, AUC, true-yes rate, true-no rate on uniform negatives, false-yes rate on the hard negatives and on the rank-2 confuser, mean top value minus accuracy, recall@5, the mean remainder and the share not read on rows of sets over 5 intents (overall and per size band), the remainder ECE per group and pool, the fitted values (T, gamma, eta, kappa with their standard errors, a, b) and the counts of calibration rows read and not read, reliability tables (the remainder's included), the raw consistency in fused units, the share of right "yes" answers at match rates 1/2, 1/5 and 1/20; per language group (EN, LTPL, lt, pl, de+sk), per pool and pool family, on strict rows, on English clinc_dev and on the pools of domain_seen_by_P; `check` with name-only intent texts; `scores` on VAL (label sets seen in training) and on the changed messages and names-only texts of the target frame (held-out clusters only); the realised split counts and the message texts shared across base pools.

The card states the shipped functions, their meaning, their held-out DEV results with intervals, the prior table of `check` (the share of right "yes" answers at the three match rates), the hard-negative rate, that 0.0 means not read, that the values sum to 1 minus the remainder and what the remainder means, the measured recall@5 and the remainder's calibration pooled and by label-set size, that they were measured on label sets of 4 to 63 intents and that above 63 the remainder is extrapolated, that the probabilities are calibrated for intents with descriptions; its full report states the rule and every result, a failed function included; no test number is in the card; the install line pins the library tag that carries the functions, published before any model upload. A failed function's method stays in the library and refuses for v1. K2 does not read the file; unchanged. With no function passed, no file is written and the release is as before.

Not another model, variant or mode under E3 (a): the same frozen weights, label texts, w 60 and k 5; no config is added. Under E3 (d): if any number of P's evaluation is published, these are published with it, whatever they show. Only for the functions in the frozen calibration file, with its parameters (sha256 pasted in the evaluation entry and pinned by the frozen manifest); nothing is fitted, chosen or changed after these numbers. After `finish()` has written the registered outputs and `pub_eval_results_manifest.json`, a phase called inside try/except that never re-raises: in the registered order, for every (set, locale) whose config p this run evaluated without a registered problem (a resume run covers only its own locales), stage A re-embeds the messages with the loaded model, takes the cached label vectors and the stored per-row candidates and cross logits of config p (rows of this run), and computes the `scores` values, the cosines of the gold and of one intent drawn from the non-gold intents of the set (seed sha256("newfx-test-neg-v1|<set>|<row id>")); stage B, only when `check` ships and after stage A covered every locale, scores the missing pairs (the gold or the drawn intent when outside the stored 5; at most 2 per row) until NEWFX_DEADLINE_H 1.35 within the unchanged `--time 01:30:00`. Metrics per set, pooled over its covered locales, with the evaluation's clusters (gold labels), 2,000 resamples, seed 0, 15 bins: for `scores` (values and remainder of (1)) top-label ECE, candidate ECE, mean top value minus accuracy, Brier, recall@5 and the remainder ECE (pooled and per size band); for `check` (gold 1/2, drawn negative 1/2, two-way weights) ECE, balanced accuracy at 0.5, true-yes rate, true-no rate, false-yes rate on the non-gold top 5, AUC and Brier; the agreement of the recomputed fused top-1 with the registered prediction; CLINC banking and credit_cards separately; no mean over sets. Files in the sibling folder `out/newfx_test_eval_<JOB>` (not under the registered run folder, and not matching the one-run guard's prefix or trace names): `newfx_test_rows_<set>.parquet`, `newfx_test_summary.csv`, `newfx_test_summary.json`, `newfx_test_results.md` (opening with: P's registered numbers were computed without calibration, RUNTIME_NOTE; these information metrics apply the frozen calibration file to the same model's scores after the registered outputs were written), `newfx_test_record.json` (P_EVAL_DONE, the calibration sha256, this entry, covered and missing locales, seconds per stage and locale, every caught error) and `newfx_test_manifest.json`; they are test-number files. Fit on the 7,081 calibration rows (7,011 of label sets over 5 intents: 6,540 with the right intent read, 471 not read): T 2.4062, gamma -1.5022, eta 0.1043, kappa 0.8009 (standard errors: 1/T 0.0073, gamma 0.335, eta 0.0148, kappa 0.0922), converged in 8 iterations. Held-out part (13,405 rows, 421 clusters; intervals by the 2.5th and 97.5th percentiles): S1 top-label ECE 0.0190 [0.0121, 0.0349]; S2 EN 0.0164, LTPL 0.0258; S3 EN_unseen 0.0324, LTPL_unseen 0.0427; S4 engine auto 99.94% of the 3,236 consistency rows within 0.01 (ECE difference -0.0002), eager 99.91% (-0.0002); S5 candidate ECE 0.0054 [0.0038, 0.0103]; S6 remainder ECE 0.0055 [0.0054, 0.0178], size bands 6 to 20 intents 0.0080, 21 to 40 0.0122, 41 or more 0.0130; the S0 flags (sums, a zero remainder on sets of 5 intents or fewer, the top value's intent equal to the fused top-1) true. Information: recall@5 0.9282 [0.9122, 0.9418]; on rows of label sets over 5 intents the right intent was not read on 7.2% and the mean remainder is 0.075; top-1 accuracy 0.7667, mean top value minus accuracy -0.0145 [-0.0313, 0.0032]; Brier 0.320.

Information: AUC 0.943; true-yes rate 0.853 and true-no rate on uniform negatives 0.893 (EN_unseen 0.916 and 0.819); the share of right yes answers at match rates 1/2, 1/5 and 1/20: 0.888, 0.665, 0.295; the false-yes rate on the hard negatives 0.420 [0.392, 0.449], on the rank-2 confuser 0.629; with name-only intent texts ECE 0.121 and balanced accuracy 0.836. So the values of `scores` are calibrated on held-out DEV intents written with descriptions; on wrapped or diacritic-free messages they understate the chance that the label is right, and with names-only intents and on MASSIVE's locales they overstate it. Batch 64 is information. Per-language latency (B-9): en, lt and pl at full k, and as information the cascade at tau 1 to 4 (k1 3) and batch 8. Long messages, the eager fallback and the top-1 agreement with the stored DEV frame are information.
